# Soundness Confidence — the cardinal-sin tracker

The **cardinal sin** is the **silent under-report**: candor reporting a function pure / effect-free when an
effect is actually reachable from it, with **no disclosure** (no `Unknown`, no `invisible`/blind/`incomplete`).
Disclosed uncertainty is safe, and over-reporting (a fabricated effect) is the opposite failure direction — a
precision failure, guarded by the per-engine fabrication probes, never to be confused with the cardinal sin;
the silent under-report is the one failure that breaks trust. This document is the **living instrument** for
answering: *how confident are we that candor never commits it, and how do we make that confidence go up over
time?*

## 1. Epistemic frame (what "eradicate" can and can't mean)

Purity over a Turing-complete language is **undecidable** (Rice's theorem) — every static analyzer has blind
spots. We therefore cannot *prove* zero cardinal sins. The achievable, trackable goal is the **conjunction**:

1. **Every known seam** (way an effect can be reached unseen) has a **standing CI gate** — so a closed sin
   can't silently regress.
2. **Dynamic ground truth** (the syscall oracle — the only evidence that catches *shared* blindness across all
   engines) covers a **growing fraction** of real-world effect-reaching code.
3. The **residual register** of known blind spots is **explicit and shrinking** — and every residual is either
   SILENT (a real cardinal-sin risk, must be driven to zero) or DISCLOSED (marked imprecision, acceptable).
4. The **find-rate** of fresh adversarial hunts trends to **zero** across diverse new seams (convergence
   evidence — never proof).

Confidence = all four, watched over time. Not a single number — a dashboard.

## 2. The cardinal-sin surface

A silent under-report lives at one intersection of **EFFECT × SEAM × ENGINE**:

- **Effects (10):** Net · Fs · Db · Exec · Env · Clock · Rand · Ipc · Clipboard · Log
- **Engines (6):** rust-scan · rust-deep · java · ts · swift · agents
- **Seams:** the *ways* an effect reaches a function without the engine attributing it. Confidence = coverage of
  this surface by standing evidence, weighted by evidence strength.

## 3. Evidence ladder (strongest → weakest)

| # | method | catches | limits | where |
|---|--------|---------|--------|-------|
| 1 | **Dynamic syscall oracle** | shared blindness (the unknown-unknown) — observed-but-not-predicted is undeniable | Fs/Net/Exec only (syscall-distinguishable); exercised paths only; Linux | `candor-rust/soundness/realworld/` (`oracle.sh`, `realworld/run.sh`) |
| 2 | **Independent-method differential** | coverage gaps (disclosed-but-unmodeled) | finds disclosed gaps, not silent ones, unless paired with #1 | ad-hoc (the 2026-06-18 coverage round) |
| 3 | **Adversarial seam probes** | a specific structural seam class | only the seams you think to probe | the per-engine regression tests + one-shot hunts |
| 4 | **Cross-engine generative matrix** | per-engine divergence on effect×indirection | WEAK for *shared* blind spots (engines can share a gap) | `candor-spec/conformance/` (`gen_differential.py`, 72 cells as of 2026-07-09) |
| 5 | **Recall corpus** | recall holes (known-semantics APIs) | only listed APIs | `candor-rust/soundness/realworld/recall/` |
| 6 | **§4 disclosure-invariant checker** | swallowed uncertainty (propagation bugs) | NOT blindness (a never-registered call is invisible to it) | `candor-spec/conformance/check_honesty.py` |
| 7 | **Seam fuzzer** | random structural shapes | shallow | `candor-rust/soundness/gen.py` |

**Key insight:** #4 (cross-engine agreement) is the weakest for the *dangerous* case — all engines sharing a
blind spot (the log-macro bug survived 4 engines + the differential for months). Real confidence needs #1
(external ground truth), not internal consensus. Grow #1.

## 4. Status scorecard — SEAM × ENGINE

Legend: 🟢 standing CI gate · 🟡 checked once / per-engine regression test only (no *cross-engine standing*
gate) · 🔴 unchecked · ⚫ known residual (see §5) · — N/A (immune by construction)

| seam class | rust-scan | rust-deep | java | ts | swift | agents |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| direct / local-call / method-recv / loop-elem / field / callback (6 basic indirections) | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢⁴ |
| key-collision (same-named unit clobber → wrong attribution) | 🟡 | — | — | 🟡 | 🟡 | 🟡 |
| **lazy-init (deferred initializer forced elsewhere)** | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | — |
| **top-level module code (load-time effect → initializer unit)** ⟨0.14⟩ | — | — | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | — |
| deferred-iterator (lazy seq built≠consumed) ³ | 🟡 | 🟢 | 🟡 | 🟡 | 🟡 | — |
| **fire-and-forget / spawned task** | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢⁴ |
| **gate-evasion / literal-masking (policy fail-closed)** | 🟢 | 🟡 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟡² |
| **implicit-conversion (effect via format/concat/interpolation)** | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | 🟢 | — |
| FFI / extern / opaque foreign call | 🟡⁷ | 🟡⁸ | 🟡⁹ | 🟡¹⁰ | 🟡¹¹ | — |
| macro / codegen reach | 🟡 | 🟢¹ | 🔴⁵ | ⚫⁶ | 🟡 | — |

¹ "🟢" here = clean/correct *by construction* (verified) — but still needs a *cross-engine standing* cell to be
truly green; today these are per-engine. ² agents = the declared-vs-observed drift gate (a different shape).
³ deferred-iterator does NOT fit the shared-compilation-unit matrix (java's whole-program CHA over
`Iterator.next()` fans out across all the cells' Iterator impls and unions every effect) — stays per-engine.
⁴ agents cannot join the shared-compilation-unit matrix (its input is a fleet definition, not code); its
analogs are CI-standing per-engine: the delegation-form chains (named / CHA / ambient + curated/uncurated
MCP sinks) are fuzzed on every push (`fuzz.py`, incl. a precision-distractor twin) and regression-pinned
(`test.py` — spawn residual, hooks matcher tiers, cron entry points) — an agent spawn IS the fleet's
fire-and-forget, and the basic-indirection analog is the delegation-form set. Lazy-init has no fleet analog
(nothing defers an initializer): N/A.
⁵ java's "—" was corrected 2026-08-27 to 🔴: `Loader.java`'s directory walk reads whatever `.class` files it's
handed, with no `generated`/annotation-processor exclusion, so a macro that rewrites BYTECODE inline (Lombok's
`@Data`/`@Builder`) is immune by construction — the rust-deep shape, verified by source inspection. But an
annotation processor that emits a SEPARATE `.java` file compiled to its own `.class` (Dagger, Room, AutoValue,
MapStruct) is a different mechanism the bytecode argument doesn't cover for free — plausible (Gradle/Maven land
those `.class` files in the same output dir the README tells users to scan) but never measured against a real
annotation-processor project. See R58.
⁶ ts's "—" was FALSE, found the same sweep as swift's row (below) — **FIXED 2026-08-28, candor-ts `0a5d493`**
(R57). A named decorator factory's effects land on its own unit, correctly (`ts.isDecorator` guards
`enclosing()` in scan.mjs on purpose, tested). An ANONYMOUS decorator expression with no top-level name
minted no unit at all: live-reproduced pre-fix, `deny Fs` over a fixture with an effectful inline decorator
exited 0 with `violations: []` — no `Unknown`, no disclosure. `localName()` now mints a position-keyed
`<decorator>@<offset>` unit for the anonymous shape before the climb reaches the guard; re-verified against
both the pre-fix and post-fix binaries in a throwaway clone, not taken on the fix's own say-so. Three
narrower, related shapes (a raw effect as a decorator argument, a closure in a named factory's argument
data, an external bodyless decorator reference) remain silent, deliberately left unfixed by the same
commit — filed as R64, not R57. See R57 and R64.
⁷ rust-scan's FFI cell was 🟡 (checked-once, no cross-engine gate) until the 2026-08-27 audit found R59
(below) — **FIXED 2026-08-28, candor-rust `3cb1906`**: a new `CALIBRATED_BUT_PARTIAL_CRATES` carves
libc/nix/rustix out of the coverage ledger's blanket `CALIBRATED_CRATES` exemption at its one consumer
site, so an unmatched call into their deliberately-unclassified generic fd verbs now discloses `invisible`
instead of vanishing; classified calls (`open`/`socket`/…) never reached the ledger at all and are
untouched. MEASURED against candor-rust HEAD: `fn drain(fd: i32) { libc::read(fd, buf, 64); }` with no
classified sibling call now reads `invisible: ["libc"]` where it previously read absent from `functions`
entirely. Corpus (1202 cached crates, standalone before/after): 97 differ (8%), every byte a new
disclosure or a previously-silent report gaining entries, zero changes to any effect field. **A same-day
follow-on audit (candor-rust `2feb264`) checked the other ~79 `CALIBRATED_CRATES` members R59's own fix
commit had left unaudited** (its stated limit at the time) and found three REAL, unrelated gaps — none of
them FFI, all three plain missing classify() rules the same silent-vanish mechanism was hiding:
`clap::Arg::env` (a real immediate `Env` read at builder time, independent of `get_matches()`'s own
classified read), `console::Term`'s raw `io::{Read,Write}` impl (`Ipc`, the same primitive `write_line`
already charges), and `arboard::{Get,Set}::file_list`/`Clear::default` (`Clipboard`, missed structurally —
the coverage-gate generator only triggers on an Fs/Net/Db/Exec self-scan signal, so a missing Clipboard
verb was invisible to it regardless of phrasing). All three fixed with concrete rules (the ambiguity that
justifies disclose-over-classify genuinely didn't apply to any of them, once checked against real vendored
source) and fixture-gated. The remaining ~76 crates were checked against real source and found complete
for this class. One filed, not fixed: `wild::ArgsOs`'s Windows-only `Iterator::next` `Fs` leak — low
reachability (invisible in the dominant `get_matches_from(wild::args_os())` idiom, structurally unseen by
the coverage-gate generator for the same reason `walkdir::IntoIter::next` needed a hand-written rule; see
R62). Direct `extern "C" { fn system(..); }` (the bindgen shape) was independently re-verified genuinely
sound throughout, unaffected by either fix — `decls.rs`'s `ForeignMod` handling records every declared name
so a call to it discloses `Unknown`/`native:extern fn`; conformance PART 79 now pins this cell, and its
byte-parity assertion against rust-deep, plus both engines' controls. Cell moves ⚫→🟡 (closed, regression +
conformance-gated, no cross-engine SEAM-matrix cell — matches java/ts's existing convention, §7b).

⁸ rust-deep's "🟢¹" (verified-by-construction) was NEVER MEASURED against the direct-extern seam and the
2026-08-27 audit found it FALSE (R60, below) — **FIXED THE SAME COMMIT as R59, candor-rust `3cb1906`**:
`Unknown`/`native:extern fn` now discloses unconditionally on `is_foreign_item()`, independent of the
crate-name-keyed `invisible` machinery a local (crate-less) foreign declaration has nothing to hang off.
MEASURED against candor-rust HEAD via `cargo dylint --lib-path`: the R60 fixture (`extern "C" { fn
system(..); } fn run_cmd() { unsafe { system(..); } }`) now reads `run_cmd`'s `inferred ==
["Unknown"]`/`unknownWhy == ["native:extern fn"]`, where the callgraph sidecar (`"run_cmd":["system"]`)
had already proven the HIR walk visited the call — the fix closes the disclosure gap, not a resolution
gap. The libc `invisible` disclosure (this cell's own already-sound mechanism, R59's fixture) is unmoved:

BOTH functions on BOTH engines and asserts the two reports are BYTE-IDENTICAL** (`inferred`/`unknownWhy`/

disclosure over routing through crate-name machinery: rust-deep's mechanism now genuinely MIRRORS
rust-scan's on this seam, not merely produces an equivalent-looking answer by a different route. Runs
outside this suite's normal build (needs the pinned nightly + `cargo-dylint`; SKIPs loudly, never silently,
on a runner with neither — see PART 79's own comment). Cell moves 🟢¹→🟡 (closed, regression +

⁹ java's FFI cell was independently measured 2026-08-27 across the three real mechanisms named in the

discloses `Unknown` (`native:<name>`, `Candor.java`'s `registerMethod`); an interface with NO implementing

(`dispatch:Lib.deleteAllFiles`); and a Panama downcall (`Linker.downcallHandle(...).invoke(...)`, built
`--enable-preview --release 21`, stable since JDK 22)
is caught by the REFLECTION disclosure path (`reflect:java.lang.foreign.SymbolLookup.find`,
`reflect:java.lang.invoke.MethodHandle.invoke`) rather than a dedicated FFI rule — sound, not by
construction-reasoning but by measurement against `build/libs/candor-java-0.33.1-all.jar` on JDK 21.
Stays 🟡 (measured sound, no cross-engine gate — this seam has no shared-compilation-unit shape, §7b).

¹¹ swift's "—" (immune by construction) was FALSE, found by the SAME 2026-08-27 audit (R61, below) and —
unlike R56's same-shaped macro finding, fixed the same day it was found — **FIXED 2026-08-28, candor-swift
`ec3e50f`**. Three independently-idiomatic C-interop mechanisms had all read silent-pure, zero disclosure:

specific real syscalls (`NATIVE_DISCLOSURE_C_FREE_FNS` — `system`/`unlink`/`mkdir`/`rename`/`fork`/

plus a `C_PLATFORM_MODULES` import check (`Darwin`/`Glibc`/`Musl`/`WinSDK`), never a denylist — the first,
unrestricted cut MEASURED 1519 false hits on swift-nio alone (`#if os(...)`/`canImport(...)` build-config
predicates, `assert`/`fatalError`, bare operators, none of them FFI), the exact false-positive class that
made an allowlist the deliberate exception to this codebase's usual denylist-over-allowlist rule; (3)
routes the `unsafeBitCast`-bound local through the SAME `opaqueFnLocals` machinery a stored opaque closure
property already uses, disclosing `Unknown`/`callback:<name>` — reused infrastructure, not new. MEASURED
against candor-swift HEAD: `import Darwin; func f() { system("rm -rf /tmp/x") }` now reads
`inferred == ["Unknown"]`/`unknownWhy == ["native:system"]` where it previously read absent from

13-package corpus and candor-swift's own source: function-count-identical outside 3 genuine new hits in
swift-syntax (a real `dlopen` in its plugin loader, a real `kill` in its CLI reducer, one more
`unsafeBitCast` site doing runtime metadata introspection — all three spot-checked against source). **The
allowlist is DELIBERATELY INCOMPLETE, stated as this fix's own limit, not a general closure claim**: an
unnamed raw Darwin/Glibc call outside `NATIVE_DISCLOSURE_C_FREE_FNS` still reads silent, the same
under-report-the-rare-case trade-off `kappaFree`'s own comment already accepts for its concrete
classification. Conformance PART 79 now pins the raw-import and `@_silgen_name` shapes plus the Process
control. Cell moves —→🟡 (closed for the allowlisted/attributed shapes, regression + conformance-gated; not
a cross-engine SEAM-matrix cell, §7b — matches java/ts's existing convention).

**Where the scorecard stands (2026-07-09; macro/codegen row corrected 2026-08-27; FFI row corrected
2026-08-27, same sweep):** 🟢 is the plurality — the basic-indirection, lazy-init, fire-and-forget,
gate-evasion and implicit-conversion rows are cross-engine-standing (§7b). The remaining 🟡/🔴/⚫ rows —
key-collision, deferred-iterator, FFI, macro — are per-engine by their nature (§7b: deferred-iterator can't
share a compilation unit across engines; FFI has no clean ts idiom; key-collision and macro are
engine-structural seams), each held by standing per-engine regression tests, plus rust-deep's gate-evasion
cell (the policy differential gates the user-facing engines; deep's policy surface stays per-engine). **The
macro/codegen row's four non-rust cells had never been independently reasoned about — the surrounding prose
only ever discussed rust's `macro_rules!`/`cfg_if!`, and "—" was pasted across java/ts/swift/agents
identically since the row's first commit.** swift's was a FALSE immunity claim (a real silent under-report,
FIXED same day the mistake was found — `dc27915`, R56); ts's was ALSO false, fixed the next day (`0a5d493`,
R57, a narrower residual filed as R64); java's
construction argument holds for one mechanism (Lombok) but is unmeasured for another (R58); agents has no
macro/codegen concept at all (a fleet definition, not source) and its "—" stands. The roadmap (§7) tracks
what's left. **The FFI row had the identical shape — four non-rust cells never independently measured, two
of them (rust-deep's "🟢¹", swift's "—") simply wrong — and the 2026-08-27 audit found: rust-scan's direct
`extern` path is genuinely sound but its `libc`-calibrated generic-fd-verb path was a live silent sin (R59);
rust-deep, the engine billed as "the sound gate" for exactly this kind of miss, silently dropped a local
`extern "C"` declaration its own callgraph proved it visited (R60) — WORSE than rust-scan on the same
mechanism; java and ts are both genuinely CLOSED across every mechanism tested (native methods / JNA-style
zero-impl dispatch / Panama for java; native addons both untyped and `.d.ts`-typed / WASM / legacy
`process.binding` for ts); swift's "—" was as false as its macro row (R56) (R61); and agents genuinely has
no FFI concept (a fleet definition has no foreign-function boundary) so its "—" stands, unlike the other
five cells which all moved.** **All three open sins (R59, R60, R61) were CLOSED the very next day
(2026-08-28)** — R59/R60 together in candor-rust `3cb1906` (one commit fixes both engines, since they were
independently broken in opposite, complementary ways on the identical fixture), R61 in candor-swift
`ec3e50f`, each verified against the fix commit rather than the author's own summary of it, and a same-day
follow-on audit of the ~79 other `CALIBRATED_CRATES` R59's own fix had left unaudited (candor-rust
`2feb264`) found and closed three further real gaps, none of them FFI (R62) — see footnote ⁷. None of the
three fixes claims general closure, stated as each fix's own limit: rust-deep's `invisible` disclosure is
still crate-name-keyed everywhere OUTSIDE the local-extern-block seam R60 closed; rust-scan's coverage
ledger still exempts the ~76 other `CALIBRATED_CRATES` members the R62 audit spot-checked and found
complete for THIS class, but did not exhaustively re-verify against every future classify() change; and
swift's `NATIVE_DISCLOSURE_C_FREE_FNS` is a deliberately incomplete allowlist — an unnamed raw Darwin/Glibc
call outside it still reads silent. Conformance PART 79 pins the R59/R60 byte-parity shape across rust-scan
and rust-deep plus swift's two allowlisted mechanisms, falsified against the pre-fix commits (candor-rust
`763e51a`, candor-swift `52d24b9`) before landing.

## 5. Residual register (known blind spots)

Each is **SILENT** (true cardinal-sin risk → drive to zero) or **DISCLOSED** (engine emits Unknown/invisible —
lower priority). Eradication = SILENT count → 0. Closed rows keep a one-line summary here; the full prose for
the essay-sized ones lives in [SOUNDNESS-LOG.md](SOUNDNESS-LOG.md).

| id | engine | residual | kind | severity | plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~R1~~ | rust-deep | implicit-conversion class — **RESOLVED 2026-06-18**: empirically already covered, not a residual | ~~SILENT~~ CLOSED | — | probe `candor-rust/ui/implicit_conversion.rs` (13-warning regression fixture) confirms all 6 sub-cases (format/Display·`?`→From·`.into()`·auto-deref·operator·Drop-glue) charge the effect + 4 pure controls stay pure. The type-aware HIR walker resolves these natively (fmt via the explicit "HOLE 2"); the scan 0.5.16 fix was the *syntactic* engine's counterpart, never needed in deep. |
| ~~R28~~ | swift | conditional conformance on a stdlib collection (`extension Array: Saveable where Element: Saveable` via `xs.persist()`) read silent-pure — two coupled gaps. **FIXED 2026-07-11** (0.8.10): (a) an array receiver edges to the local `Array.<member>` extension unit via a soft resolveQual edge (std methods drop silently, no spurious Unknown); (b) a bare `forEach { $0.method() }` over self types `$0` as the extension's `where Element: P` bound (FnInfo.selfElementType + a selfElementStack). Pure conditional conformance stays pure. | ~~SILENT low~~ CLOSED | — | SOUNDNESS-LOG.md, 2026-07-11 R28 entry; gate `testConditionalConformanceOnArrayCollectionDispatches` |

| R53 | rust-scan | **trait-qualified UFCS call reads pure** (fix ATTEMPTED then REVERTED on code review — the typed `T::method` edge could resolve to T's INHERENT `method` shadowing a trait default → fabrication; no cheap gate, low value). A/B zero concrete over-fire ~2600 fns; : `Runner::go(&t)` and `<Task as Runner>::go(&t)` (t: Task, `impl Runner for Task` effectful) drop silent — the INHERENT form `Task::go(&t)` resolves, but the TRAIT-qualified form doesn't (no fn named `Runner::go`; the impl is `Task::go`). FABRICATION SUBTLETY: unlike `dyn Runner` dispatch (runtime type unknown → CHA-over-all-impls is the sound over-approximation), a UFCS call's receiver type is STATICALLY known (the first arg / the `<T as ..>` type), so CHA-over-all-impls would OVER-CHARGE a multi-impl trait (edging B::go/C::go onto a call that only hits Task::go). Safe pre-designed fix: resolve via the first-arg type (`&t`→Task→`Task::go`), OR edge only when the trait has EXACTLY ONE local impl (unambiguous, the R36-default discipline). Deferred: low incidence (4–8 `<T as Trait>::` sites per large crate, nearly all std-trait disambiguation — `Future`/`From`/`Default` — which candor leaves as external; a LOCAL effectful-trait UFCS target is rare). | SILENT (open) | low/niche | real but rare; the fix must stay precise (arg-typed or single-impl) — never CHA-all a statically-known-receiver call, that fabricates |

| ~~R49~~ | rust-scan | **a local effectful-`Drop` guard held as a struct FIELD** — constructing an owner `struct Session { _g: Guard }` (or `Vec<Guard>`, or a nested owner) runs `Guard::drop` at the owner's scope exit, but reads silent-pure. The DIRECT case (a local OF the drop-type) IS charged; only the field/transitive case is missed (rust analog of swift's R33 deinit-glue, field edition). **[CORRECTED 2026-08-31 — that sentence is MEASURABLY FALSE as a general claim, and this row's CLOSED status is narrower than it reads. See R66.](#r66)** The field cell IS genuinely closed at HEAD (verified by execution: field owner via assoc ctor, `Vec<Guard>` field, nested owner, field-literal-in-a-`let` all charged) — but only along the two routes that existed when it was written. "The DIRECT case IS charged" holds for `Guard::new()` and for `Guard { .. }` bound to a `Pat::Ident`, and is FALSE for a TUPLE-STRUCT (newtype) guard in every position including the bound local, and false for a brace-literal guard in 16 of 21 executed positions. Measured: `struct BGuard{id}; let _g = BGuard{id};` → `['Fs']`; `struct TGuard(String); let _g = TGuard("x");` → ABSENT. `eat(Session { _g: Guard{..} })` and `let (_s,_n) = (Session{..}, 1)` are also silent — the closure inherits the same syntax/binder limits it was built on. **A prototype fix (a transitive drop-owner closure over `drop_types` × `fields`/`field_elem`, no new cache plumbing) was BUILT + regression-tested green, then REVERTED on the A/B gate**: it fabricated **14 false `Unknown`s on flate2** (`Compress::new`/`Decompress::new`/… — constructors that CONSTRUCT AND RETURN the owner, whose owned `Stream`'s FFI Drop runs in the CALLER's scope, not the constructor's). Field-owners are overwhelmingly constructed-to-be-RETURNED (resource wrappers), so the returned-value escape case DOMINATES this vein — unlike direct guards (constructed-to-be-used-locally), where the same over-approximation is rare/accepted. A correct fix needs a returned-value ESCAPE GATE (swift-R33 style), which needs return-type info not cheaply available (FnInfo carries none; the `returns` index is leaf-keyed so `::new` collides and drops as ambiguous) — i.e. a new FnInfo return-type field or collector-side escape analysis. **Zero corpus incidence for direct effects** (dozens of crates; the only real local-Drop crate, flate2, flushes to a generic writer, not a classified effect). | ~~SILENT~~ CLOSED | — | **FIXED 2026-07-18 (`3e2a52c`)**: transitive drop-owner closure + a RETURN-ESCAPE gate (conservative membership check on `FnInfo.ret_idents`, sound under flate2's leaf-name collisions; `-> Self` resolved). ADDITIVE-only (direct case untouched → no under-reports); A/B zero concrete fabrication + zero removed across flate2/syn/h2/tokio-util/…; regression owns_field/nested/vec charged, make_* escape-gated |

Two arms held constant — effect in a called method, and **effect in the constructor itself** (the faithful translation of the swift shape). Covered: receiver · argument · array literal · `List.of` · ternary · try-with-resources · resource-from-a-CALL · instance and static initializer · nested/chained · anonymous inner class · switch subject · `assert` · catch · finally · indy string-concat · `synchronized` subject · array index · for init and update · popped return value · `&&` right operand · **throw operand** · **`super(...)` argument** · return position · cast/`instanceof` operand. Widened past its own trigger: `--release 8` (synthetic `access$NNN` nest bridges charged, and callers charged THROUGH them), `-g:none` (**byte-identical** charged set — receiver typing does not depend on debug info), covariant-return bridges, and descriptor-keyed unit identity (an effectful/pure overload pair does not name-merge). Over-charge control: 11 units correctly pure, the sharpest being a method that CONSTRUCTS in return position and is pure because the ctor is pure — candor tracks the call, not the construction. **THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE SPEC, which is the valuable part: a conformance row phrased "construction position must not change attribution" is NOT HOSTABLE ON THE REFERENCE ENGINE IN A DISCRIMINATING WAY.** java passes it 44/44 BY CONSTRUCTION, so the row would be green whether or not the engine were correct — exactly the "passes with and without the fix" shape this family kills rows for. **So R65/R66 are a SOUNDNESS VEIN, NOT A SPEC RUNG**, and R65's "write the row before the port" debt is discharged by this measurement rather than by shipping an undiscriminating row. **The row that IS hostable four-way**, surfaced by the same agent's Net arm: *literal-surface visibility must not depend on the operand's DISTANCE FROM THE SINK, or the loss must be disclosed* — every engine has a literal-window analogue, and the arms discriminate on java today (host literal adjacent to the sink → `Llm, Net` with `hosts`; the same compile-time-constant host ONE CALL HOP away → bare `Net`, no host, `incomplete: ["Net"]`). **A HYPOTHESIS THAT DIED, recorded so nobody re-raises it:** the agent tried hard to make that bare-`Net` case a cardinal sin and could not — SPEC §1 ⟨0.13⟩ makes bare `Net` the CONTRACT for an unknown host ("never guessed"), and AS-EFF-008 quantifies over `e ∈ I(f)` as a PRECONDITION, so `Llm ∉ I(f)` means the rule correctly does not fire. It also withdrew its own attack-K claim against `Policy.java:865` after building the case that comment describes and measuring it fail closed correctly. | CLEAN NEGATIVE | a genuine residual needing judgement, not a fix: `--gate-json` carries only `{spec, ok, analyzed, violations}`, so the per-function `incomplete` marker does NOT ride the machine channel (SPEC §2244 says it is explicitly not guaranteed to), and `deny <E>` never consults `surfaceIncomplete` — only `allow <E>` does. So `deny Llm` over a one-hop-constant host is `ok:true` exit 0 with no signal, while `allow Net <hosts>` fails closed | no fix owed. UNEXAMINED and stated rather than implied: Kotlin/Scala/Groovy front-ends (javac only; Kotlin's `inline`/coroutine lowering is a genuinely different position set), ECJ-compiled bytecode, and whether the Net/Llm literal-window result transfers to `allow Exec`/`allow Fs`/`allow Db` — measured for Net/Llm only, and by this file's own boundary rule that is not assumed to transfer |

An ordinary, common Swift shape. Both confirmed by EXECUTION with unbuffered `deinit` markers, not by reasoning. **FIXED 2026-08-31 (`14db525`)** with a bounded `guaranteedToEscape` path check that defaults to "not proven → CHARGE" for anything unmodelled (switch, loops, do/catch). **AND THE FIX'S OWN FIRST DRAFT INTRODUCED AN O(2^N) BLOWUP, caught by TIMING rather than by reading:** it threaded its continuation as an `@escaping () -> Bool` closure invoked from both arms of every `if`, so `PrivacyManifestCLI.swift`'s long `if usage == "…"` table took the engine's self-scan from **3 seconds to 9+ minutes** — and all 988 tests stayed GREEN throughout, because nothing times a self-scan. Fixed by computing each continuation eagerly as a plain `Bool`; self-scan back to 0.2–2.8s, now pinned by a 500-`if`/`else` test with an explicit elapsed-time assertion. | ~~SILENT~~ CLOSED | over-charge controls written first and all held: guard-else before construction, else-if chains where every arm returns the same binding, nested closures with returns, tuple-destructuring returns | commit candor-swift `14db525`; `DeinitGlueConditionalEscapeProcessTests`; 989/989, smoke 148/0, four-way conformance OK **re-run on clean trees** (its first run read candor-rust's uncommitted tree — see CLAUDE.md on conformance reading working trees). A/B over 6 real packages / ~11,300 fns: zero rows removed, zero changes attributable to the fix. **THE HEADLINE FOR THE FAMILY: one question — "does your escape gate suppress on SOME-path escape?" — found a cardinal sin in TWO engines on the same day, in neither case via a test suite, and in swift's case one that had been shipping for months.** |

The DIRECT-field form `(self.cb.as_ref().unwrap())()` is honest (`Unknown`/`unresolved:true`/`callback:unresolved call`), so the two spellings of the same operation disagree. **Root cause — AMENDED, because the first diagnosis was wrong and the fixing agent attacked it.** The original filing said `resolve_elem_trait_leaves` returns EMPTY for an `Option<Box<dyn Fn()>>` field so the binding falls through untyped. MEASURED by instrumenting the built binary: it returns `["Fn"]` and the binding DOES land in `trait_vars`. The real gap is a two-table split — `trait_vars` feeds only the `.method()`-call CHA/dispatch resolver, while `visit_expr_call`'s call-SYNTAX resolver consults only `fn_typed_vars`. A Fn-family binding therefore stops at `trait_vars`, is live, and is simply never consulted at its actual invocation site. A plausible mechanism that reads correctly and is false is the most expensive kind of filing; it survived because it explained the symptom. The direct-field form skips that path and hits the honest catch-all at `collector.rs:1453`. **THE SCOPED BYPASS, EXECUTED and independently reproduced by the coordinator** on `fx5_scoped_policy` (ground truth: the write fires every run): `deny Fs` → exit 1, 3 violations; `deny Fs construct` → exit 1, 1 violation (both calibrations alive); **`deny Fs dispatch` → exit 0, 0 violations** over the function that actually invokes the closure. `show`/`path`/`impact dispatch` all answer "no function matching", while `callers Holder::go` DOES find `dispatch` via the separate call-graph cache — the unit is known to exist and the effect layer treats it as pure. Caller-attribution, the R65/R66 class. **DISTINCT from the ⟨0.35⟩ CHA-completeness clause** and found while falsifying it: rust CANNOT host that clause (see below), and this is what the investigation turned up instead. **FIXED 2026-09-01 (candor-rust `3cf055d`)**: a `leaves_are_callable` helper hedges every Fn/FnMut/FnOnce-leaved binding into `fn_typed_vars`, applied at EVERY binding-producing position found by grepping all `Bound::Traits`/`trait_vars.insert` sites — if-let, match arm, for-loop, let-else, HOF closure param, tuple destructure (both forms), and `while let`, which had NO handler for any trait shape at all. A/B over 256 crates: **zero regressions, 60 function-effect changes all in the safe direction**, and real unforced instances of this bug in `futures-executor`, `serde-untagged` and **`url`** — one of the most-depended-upon crates in the ecosystem. **MY ACCEPTANCE TEST WAS WRONG AND THE AGENT REFUSED IT:** I demanded `deny Fs dispatch` exit 1 post-fix; it does not, and must not, because that would fabricate a literal `Fs` claim on a function whose real effect a syntactic scan cannot see. The sound signal is `deny Unknown dispatch`, exit 0 → 1, calibrations unchanged — verified independently. One gap left OPEN and documented: a closure carried as a bare enum tuple-variant payload, which needs a new Pass-A index. | ~~SILENT~~ CLOSED | the direct-field spelling was always honest, so the exposure was the binding positions only | extend the fn-typed detection that already exists for plain `let` (`expr_is_fn_typed`, `collector.rs:1028`) to the binding-producing positions — `visit_expr_if`, `visit_expr_match`, and by the same audit-boundary rule closure params and for-loop vars sharing the shape — keyed on `is_callable_type` rather than on CHA-leaf-bearing dispatch traits. An existing SPEC §4 property (unresolved callback → Unknown) already shipped elsewhere in the same file; an implementation gap in one binding position, NOT a new cross-engine clause |

Three executed attempts to instantiate that toggle on rust all failed: named-impl dispatch at 0→1→2 impls (0 → honest `Unknown`; 1 → `Fs`; **2, adding a pure sibling → still `Fs`, with `calls` listing BOTH impls** — a union, not a pick); a trait default method (charges correctly); and a blanket impl over an `Fn` bound (pure at both counts — a separate gap, noted below, not a toggle). **Mechanism, read from source:** `collector.rs:1771-1786` resolves local-trait dispatch by iterating EVERY locally-visible impl and pushing an edge for each while `impls.len() <= 12`; zero or >12 is the only route to `unresolved`. That is structurally the OPPOSITE of resolving to a single candidate, and you cannot `impl Fn` on stable, so the defining A/B cannot be constructed. **Exemption by CONSTRUCTION, not an unported MUST** — PART 87 records it as such so it is not re-filed as a gap. NOT ATTEMPTED and stated: `#[derive]`-generated impls of a user trait (syn does not expand proc-macros). | R73 | swift | **a method call whose RECEIVER IS A MODULE-SCOPE `let`/`var` resolves its call-graph edge to the GLOBAL'S OWN BARE IDENTIFIER instead of to the method — so the calling function collapses to empty and vanishes from `functions[]`.** Five lines of ordinary Swift, no protocol, no closure, no lambda: `final class Worker { func doWork() { <writes a file> } }` · `let worker = Worker()` · `func invoke() { worker.doWork() }`. INDEPENDENTLY REPRODUCED by the coordinator from scratch. So this is CALLER ATTRIBUTION — it defeats scoped and layered policies, `path`, `gains`, `tour` and `fix-gate`, i.e. the reachability surface this product sells, while a blanket deny is untouched. Over-charge control clean across every cell: the failure mode is pure under-report, never fabrication. | SILENT (open) | unscoped deny unaffected; failure is confined to caller attribution | fix receiver resolution so a module-scope `let`/`var` receiver resolves to the METHOD, not the global identifier — and enumerate the neighbouring receiver positions (instance properties `self.singleton.method()`, cross-file and cross-package globals, existential-array elements dispatched in a loop) rather than the three that were measured. ## 6. The metric (track these four; each "step forward" moves one)

1. **Cardinal-surface coverage** = % of (seam × engine) cells at 🟢 in §4. *As of 2026-07-09: the
   cross-engine matrix holds **72** vs-ground-truth cells (9 indirections/seams × 8 effects — the growth
   history 30 → 48 → 64 → 72 is §7b's narrative), 4 of 6 seam classes are cross-engine-standing (§7b), and
   strict mode (G1/G2) makes the gate un-foolable. The rust-deep column is 🟢 except its gate-evasion cell
   (the masked-literal differential gates the user-facing scan/java/ts/swift gates; deep's policy surface
   stays per-engine); the remaining 🟡 rows are per-engine by nature (§4 note). Target: every closed seam →
   🟢 via the matrix's SEAM axis where it fits, standing per-engine gates where it doesn't.*
2. **Oracle coverage** = # real crates × effects under dynamic ground truth (§3 #1). *2026-06-18: grew 7→11
   crates — Net ×3 (std/minreq/ureq), Exec ×3 (duct/xshell/std), Fs ×4 (fs-err/std/walkdir/tempfile) — now
   ≥3 per syscall-distinguishable effect (Fs/Net/Exec); incl. the walkdir calibration confirmed vs the kernel
   + tempfile as a disclosure probe. + 13 crates with an UNCALIBRATED disclosure probe per
   effect (minreq/subprocess/fs_extra — a crate candor doesn't model, exercised for real; the strongest test).
   Rand/Clock are syscall-distinguishable but markerless/noisy (getrandom has no string arg; HashMap seeds
   getrandom) — covered by the non-syscall recall complement instead. **NOW CONTINUOUS: `realworld-oracle.yml`
   runs on every push to main + every PR (was workflow_dispatch) → kernel ground truth is a STANDING gate, a
   new silent under-report on a real crate fails CI.** And the NON-SYSCALL RECALL complement now covers the
   last 2 effects (Env, Clock — recall corpus 14→20 cases, all sound), so with the syscall oracle (Fs/Net/
   Exec) candor's effect classification is under ground-truth/known-semantics coverage for ALL 10 EFFECTS.
   Recall is also wired into realworld-oracle.yml → BOTH ground-truth methods are now continuous standing
   gates. 2026-06-18: moved the session's adversarial-sweep finds UP the evidence ladder to ground truth —
   syscall oracle 13→**14 drivers** (added `fs_writefmt`: a custom `fmt::Write` writing a marker file via
   `write!`; CI-verified `ran=1 effect=Fs candor=[Fs] certain` — the write-fmt class now KERNEL-gated, the
   strongest evidence, independent of engine logic); recall 20→**23** (seam_lazy_force/seam_thread_local/
   seam_write_fmt, all →Clock). realworld-oracle.yml run GREEN: 14 sound / 0 under-reports / 0 fabrications,
   recall 23 sound. So the systemic write-fmt shared blind spot is now caught by EXTERNAL ground truth, not
   just engine-internal fixtures. NEXT: more uncalibrated recall probes; deepen each effect's real-crate
   diversity.*
3. **Open SILENT residuals** (§5) = count by severity. *As of 2026-07-11 this read "**zero FIXABLE
   silent residuals remain**" — and the **2026-07-18 cross-engine dispatch era FALSIFIED that** (the §1
   point, live): a fresh systematic hunt of the trait-object/dispatch vein found **~20 fixable cardinal
   sins across all four engines** (R32–R44 in the LOG), every one now FIXED + regression-gated + corpus-
   A/B'd + four-way-conformance-clean. So "zero fixable remain" is never a provable state, only "none
   found by the hunts run so far." After that wave: the open residuals are again the FUNDAMENTAL syntactic
   limits (R2–R8, low/v.low) PLUS a NEW niche dispatch long-tail — blanket impls `impl<T: B> Ext for T`
   (fabrication-risk to auto-resolve; low value), nested `Vec<Option<Box<dyn>>>`, tuple-destructured dyn
   returns, `Default::default()` turbofish, and a ts super-interface PRECISION gap (reads sound Unknown,
   not a cardinal sin) — all characterized in the LOG (2026-07-18 long-tail entry), niche, none med+.
   **2026-08-25 adds two of a DIFFERENT KIND, and the distinction is worth keeping: R54 (`diff`) and R55
   (`receipt`) are not blind spots in the CLASSIFIER but verbs that read a report correctly and then decline
   to pass on what it says about itself.** Every residual above is "the engine could not see it"; these two
   are "the engine saw it and the answer does not carry it" — reached by driving the VERB SURFACE rather
   than the effect surface, which is an axis this register had not been swept along before. **Both CLOSED
   2026-08-26**: R54 by porting `gains`' PREFIXED dual-locator shape (candor-java `1fdf0f2`, candor-ts
   `9790ce3`, candor-rust `f3bedac`); R55 by a new TSV `incomplete\ttrue` row, tie-broken by reading the
   format's real consumer rather than guessing (candor-rust `873f178` — the only engine shipping `receipt`
   at all; java/ts have none, swift has no query verbs).
   Target: 0 med+; lows/niche documented-accepted; keep hunting fresh veins (the count is a floor, not a proof).*
4. **Find-rate** = cardinal sins found per fresh adversarial round. *Lede (as of 2026-07-18): SIX find
   eras — the 6th is the **cross-engine dispatch era** (2026-07-18, autonomous, part in PARALLEL via
   subagents): a systematic sweep of the trait-object / dynamic-dispatch vein across ALL FOUR engines found
   ~20 cardinal sins — R32 (a PROVIDED method driving a required OVERRIDE, four-way + a real jsoup Clock
   recovery), R33–R35 (swift deinit-glue / generic-operator / @dynamicCallable), R36 (rust trait-default →
   requirement, java/ts already sound), R37/R37b/R40 (rust dyn-collection: param / generic bound / field),
   R38 (java unbound interface-method-ref), R39 (swift generic-array), R41 (container/Option dispatch —
   HashMap values, Arc<Mutex> guard chains, Option/Result unwrap in every form; rust + swift IN PARALLEL,
   real pgman Log recovery), R42/R44 (rust method returning a bare/collection trait object), R43 (supertrait
   / super-protocol dispatch, rust + swift in parallel). Every one fixed + regression-gated + corpus-A/B'd +
   four-way-conformance-clean, all on 0.22. This era MASSIVELY re-opened the find-rate — the 5th (swift-
   resolution) era's "dropping toward zero" was premature for the SHARED dispatch vein, which had a deep
   systematic hole in every engine (the exact cross-engine-agreement blind spot §1 warns of: engines agreeing
   on the wrong PURE answer). After it, fresh NON-dispatch probes (closures / threads / channels / iterator
   adapters / std-I/O classification / stdio) came back SOUND — the dispatch vein is now saturated; the era's
   find-rate is dropping. Earlier five eras — seam-class, κ-coverage, porcelain, coverage, swift-resolution —
   every find fixed and standing-gated; convergence NOT reached, and each era shift re-opens the find-rate.
   That is the epistemic frame (§1) working as designed: two parallel subagent branches even had their OWN
   corpus-A/B gates catch over-fires mid-flight (R43-swift's 139-fn Unknown blast, self-corrected). The
   instrument's job is to make each new era's finds cheap and standing-gated, not to declare victory.*
   - *Inheritance-of-accessors probe (2026-07-10): 1 find — swift inherited property accessors (computed
     getter / `didSet` / subscript) read silent-pure through a subclass while inherited METHODS climbed
     (R22, fixed 0.8.7, gated). A "covered seam, uncovered edition" find: the accessor climb existed for
     the own type and the method climb existed for inheritance, but their intersection did not. candor-ts +
     java were probed with the same shape and are sound — swift-specific, not shared. Prose: LOG.*
   - *Operator-overload / setter probe (2026-07-10): 1 find — swift setter `newValue` read silent-pure
     (an effect through the implicit value param, `set { newValue.write(…) }`; computed-property + subscript
     setters + `willSet`), because `newValue` was never typed (R23, fixed 0.8.8, gated). The operator paths
     themselves — `==`, `+`, subscript-getter — were sound; ts/kotlin/rust use explicit typed setter params
     (no implicit `newValue`), so swift-specific. Another "covered seam, uncovered edition": accessor bodies
     were collected, but the setter's implicit param wasn't given the type that lets its member calls
     resolve. Prose: LOG.*
   - *Error-path / cleanup-block probe (2026-07-10): 0 finds — an effect that runs ONLY in a `finally` /
     `catch` / Swift `defer`/`guard-else` / Rust error combinator / implicit try-with-resources `close()`
     is charged by all four engines (they walk the full statement tree; the cleanup path is an ordinary
     call edge). Java's try-with-resources — an effect via a compiler-synthesized `close()` — is the
     sharpest case and is sound (the close is real bytecode). Convergence evidence, not promoted to a
     standing gate. Prose: LOG.*
   - *Seam-class era (2026-06-18 → 06-21, rounds 1–17): ~14 finds, all fixed + gated. Highlights: the
     lazy-init forcing site (rust-deep `8bf9c6b`), the agents named-delegation narrowing (a prompt mention
     is not a spawn-set proof, `755216a`), thread_local (R13), and the write-fmt writer side (R14/R16) — a
     SYSTEMIC blind spot silent in four engines at once, the exact case cross-engine agreement hides; since
     then a find in one engine triggers a sweep of all. Rounds 7, 8, 12 and 13 found 0. Validated on real
     code the same era (the PetClinic end-to-end gate, the gson InetAddress catch). Full round-by-round
     narrative: SOUNDNESS-LOG.md, the seam-class-era entry.*
   - *κ-coverage and porcelain eras (2026-06-21 → 2026-07-09; full prose per entry in SOUNDNESS-LOG.md;
     register entries R18–R21): real-app dogfooding found the inherited-into-project silent-pure vein and
     closed the CLASS (batches 25–27, R18); the uflexi legacy round mined batches 28–31 to a zero ledger and
     found one live silent-Net member gap (Jackson, batch 30b); the breadth invited six shipped classifier
     regressions (0.8.4 review patch, R19 — caught by review, now moving under scheduled CI); the same
     covered-module shape then surfaced in swift (UserDefaults/Keychain/Bundle, R20); and the 2026-07-09
     whole-project review opened a THIRD find category — porcelain/output-channel fail-opens (R21), plus one
     normative contradiction (AS-EFF-008's opaque case: spec text vs the conformance-pinned fail-closed
     behavior — the written contract had lagged the machine-checked one since the 0.5.15 hardening). All
     fixed; chaining + stale-baseline became conformance PARTs 14–15 the same day, and PART 14's first run
     caught candor-scan's missing empty-report ledger exemption.*
   - *Coverage era (2026-07-10): the first-ever coverage measurement made "documented surface with zero
     executions" a find category of its own — four real bugs, including a broken user-facing gate
     (candor-java's CANDOR_STRICT checkConformance had never been executed by any harness). See the LOG's
     coverage-wave entry and §8.*

## 7. Roadmap (meaningful, measurable steps)

1. **Standing gates (highest leverage):** extend `conformance/gen_differential.py` from 6 indirections to the
   full seam set (each seam × effect × engine = a CI cell). Converts the 🟡 one-shot hunts to 🟢; a regression
   becomes un-shippable. *DONE for the classes the matrix can hold (2026-06-18, §7b): 4 of 6 seam classes are
   cross-engine-standing; deferred-iterator + FFI stay per-engine by nature.*
2. **Grow the dynamic oracle (strongest evidence):** add real crates per effect to `soundness/realworld/`,
   wire `realworld-oracle.yml` to run in CI on every push. *DONE 2026-06-18 (metric #2): 14 oracle drivers +
   23 recall cases run on every candor-rust push. Growth (more crates/effects) remains open-ended.*
3. **Eradicate SILENT residuals (§5):** R1 done (already covered); drive the remaining R2–R8 (all low/v.low) to
   zero or convert to disclosed-Unknown. *Each = a step on metric #3.*
4. **rust-deep parity + unblock its self-guard (R12):** the deep engine must carry every scan fix and
   be continuously gated. *Self-guard part DONE (R12 closed 2026-07-09): the nightly pin moved past the ICE,
   the Self-guard step + `realworld-oracle-deep.yml` run in CI on every push, and `nightly-bump.yml` automates
   future bumps.*
5. **agents seam battery (R11):** run the six seam classes against the agents drift model. *DONE 2026-06-18
   (round 10, R11): one find (named-delegation narrowing), fixed + CI-gated; the §4 agents column now reflects
   it (footnote ⁴).*
6. **Convergence log:** record each adversarial round's find-rate in [SOUNDNESS-LOG.md](SOUNDNESS-LOG.md), one
   entry per round (§6 metric 4 keeps the compressed view); a sustained zero across *diverse* new seams is the
   strongest convergence signal we can have.

## 7b. Gate integrity — the gate itself was code-reviewed + hardened (2026-06-18)

Before extending the gate we reviewed *the gate*. The core is sound: the generative matrix compares each
engine against a **hardcoded EXPECTED effect** (vs-ground-truth, not mere inter-engine agreement — so it
catches even a *shared* blind spot), absent-fn → PURE → DROP → fails, and the callback accepted-band tolerates
`Unknown` but never silent-pure. Three findings, all addressed:

- **G1 (fixed) — silent engine-skip → false multi-engine green.** A skipped (absent) ts/swift engine left the
  verdict "OK" with fewer engines; a misconfigured CI could read a 2-engine pass as a 4-engine guarantee.
  FIX: `CONFORMANCE_REQUIRE_ALL=1` strict mode — a skipped engine now FAILS (run.sh Parts 6/6c + gen_differential).
  Verified: strict + engine absent → exit ≠ 0; strict + all present → exit 0.
- **G2 (fixed) — `check_honesty.py` silently degraded** when the callgraph sidecar was missing (fell back to
  inline `calls`, which misses pure-fn callers — the dangerous case). FIX: strict mode FAILS on a missing
  callgraph. Verified.
- **G3 (in progress) — coverage was the real limit.** The matrix is vs-ground-truth, so every (effect×seam)
  cell it covers is strongly gated and every one it doesn't is ungated. Extended the EFFECT axis 5→8
  (added **Rand/Db/Log**, the proven-cross-engine vocab; matrix 30→48 cells, all engines agree). **Ipc/Clipboard
  stay out by design** (no JDK std IPC primitive; no node clipboard model — structurally per-engine). Remaining
  G3 work = the SEAM axis (add renderers for lazy-init / deferred-iterator / fire-and-forget / implicit-conversion
  / gate-masking / FFI to the matrix) — the next roadmap increment. **STARTED: implicit-conversion +
  fire-and-forget are now matrix INDIRECTIONS (matrix 48→64 cells, all 4 engines agree, exact {effect}) —
  those two seam rows are now 🟢 cross-engine-standing. THEN lazy-init too (matrix 64→72, all 4 agree) → 3 of 6
  seam classes are now 🟢.** The other 3 don't fit the EFFECT matrix: deferred-iterator (java whole-program CHA
  over a shared `Iterator` interface unions every cell's effect — footnote ³); gate-masking (a POLICY-verdict
  seam — extend the policy differential instead); FFI (expected is {Unknown}/disclosure, no clean ts idiom).
  So the matrix seam-axis is effectively COMPLETE for the classes it can hold; the residual 3 stay per-engine
  (🟡) by their nature, documented here. THEN gate-masking too, via a SEPARATE policy-verdict differential
  (`gen_masking.py`, also wired into run.sh): for each literal-surface effect {Net→host, Exec→cmd, Fs→path,
  Db→table}, a MASKED denied literal beside a benign one must FAIL the `allow <Effect> <benign>` gate
  (fail-closed) in every engine, and the compliant program must PASS — 16 (effect×engine) cells, all green.
  Building it SURFACED a real swift gate-evasion: `shellOut(to: runtimeVar)` (ShellOut, classified Exec) was
  missing from the Exec establishing set, so a masked command evaded `allow Exec` — FIXED (candor-swift 0.5.21,
  `1a60bce`). So **4 of 6 seam classes are now cross-engine-standing** (implicit-conv/fire-forget/lazy-init via
  the effect matrix; gate-masking via the policy differential); only deferred-iterator (java CHA artifact) and
  FFI (expected {Unknown}, no clean ts idiom) remain per-engine by nature.

**CI action item — DONE (2026-07-09):** conformance.yml now carries a macos four-engine job running with
`CONFORMANCE_REQUIRE_ALL=1` (all four toolchains provisioned), alongside the fast ubuntu three-way leg and a
weekly released-artifacts leg. Strict mode is a standing CI property, no longer local-only.

## 8. How to read confidence today (2026-07-10)

- **Floor is solid and continuously gated:** the §4 disclosure invariant (never silent-pure where an effect
  is reached) is a standing conformance part over every engine's own report; the kernel syscall oracle +
  recall corpus run on every candor-rust push; the cross-engine matrix holds 72 vs-ground-truth cells;
  the masked-literal policy verdict, gate-verdict/exit agreement, `.candor/config`, chaining and the
  stale-baseline posture are all standing four-way differentials (conformance PARTs 12–15).
- **The find-rate has NOT converged** — and the finds have changed shape. The seam-class era (rounds
  1–17, ~14 finds, all fixed) gave way to the κ-coverage era: real-app dogfooding keeps surfacing
  covered-namespace member gaps (Panache, the inherited-into-project class, the legacy-enterprise tier,
  Jackson's live silent-Net, swift's UserDefaults/Keychain) — and breadth-work invites its own
  regressions (the 0.8.4 review patch: six classifier regressions shipped in 0.8.3, caught by review,
  not CI). The 2026-07-09 whole-project review added a third era: the *porcelain* — fail-opens in the
  layers users invoke (cargo-candor policy/guard, gate-json write paths, deps resolution) that the
  engine-level fail-closed doctrine never swept. And the 2026-07-10 coverage wave added a fourth:
  *documented surfaces with zero executions* — the first-ever coverage measurement showed several
  documented, load-bearing gate surfaces had never been executed by any harness, and pinning them found
  four real bugs, including a broken user-facing gate (candor-java's CANDOR_STRICT `checkConformance`).
- **So: high and rising on the analysis core; the active frontier is κ-coverage member gaps,
  gate-surface fail-opens, and never-executed surfaces — each now with standing gates.** Treat any new
  framework's inherited-into-project shape, any new output/auxiliary channel, and any documented surface
  with zero executions as guilty until gated.

A/B 6 repos zero losses / zero non-initializer gains; held-out `proper-lockfile` 1 violation → 0. Concept is right in the reference engine — with the dep INSIDE the scanned set candor-java gives `app.App.<clinit> { Env* }` one hop via `dep.Dep.<clinit>`; outside it, dropped silently. Blanket disclosure MEASURED AND REJECTED (60–100% of modules across 6 packages, candor-ts itself 83%). Resolution is DETERMINATION not disclosure: chain the dep report (`deps`) or scan dependency initializers only. **swift NOT N/A — the vein is LIVE there** (globals are lazy, so a READ forces the initializer; `globalReads` exists but is skipped when the global is the base of a member access — the commonest shape). The two-line fix recovers the fixtures but produced **113 gains / 226 fns** on candor-swift's own tree from module-flat name collisions (`pipe`/`run`/`env` locals matching same-named fixture globals) → **A/B-REVERTED**. Two further attempts also reverted, and the ORDER is the finding: module-scoping cut fabrications 113→94; a file-scope guard (a real defect — a `let` inside a top-level `if`/`for` block is a LOCAL, and candor-swift registered it as a global; `<main>` unchanged so nothing lost) removed the rest but RE-SHUFFLED which colliding declaration wins (`DeclCollector.pushType`, a 3-line array append, went pure → `{Env,Fs,Unknown}`). **Root cause: global units are keyed by BARE NAME.** Required order: (a) unique qualified keying, **(b) file-scope guard — SHIPPED `080fb3e`, A/B'd in isolation: 0 gains on candor-swift (9 effectful phantoms dropped) + swift-syntax (6), `<main>` unchanged, type members untouched**, **(c) DONE `acfed07`** — reading a MEMBER of a global (`cfg.count`, `analysis.allFns`, `SYNTAX_NODES.map{}`) forces its initializer; exclusions for the member NAME and for same-named INSTANCE PROPERTIES keep it exact. 26 gains / 0 losses on candor-swift + 7/0 on swift-syntax, and **all sampled gains TRACED to genuine global-to-global reads**, not assumed. Three reverts, each right at the time: 113 fabrications → 34 (unique identity) → 26 (self-property exclusion) → all genuine. **(a) is NOT edge filtering** — a 2-module fixture shows two same-named file-scope globals **MERGE into one unit** carrying the union of their effects, and a reader of one inherits the other's (`utilUses` gains `Fs` from a different module) — a LIVE fabrication today, independent of the vein. Filtering removes it but also removes the reader's genuine `Env` (fabrication → silent under-report, wrong trade) and is a no-op post-(b). (a) = module-qualified global unit IDENTITY so they do not merge; spec-visible. rust SOUND (LazyLock force, `main { Env }`), java SOUND (both `<clinit>` and method forms). | effect mechanisms that die at the SCAN BOUNDARY | 2026-07-25 | **ALL FOUR ENGINES (OPEN; rust 3 of 5 fixed — `1623a07` stringification, `a2fbe74` drop glue, `50218e3` interfaceUnion in `--deps`; java/ts/swift in progress)** | **FALSE ALL-CLEAR ON A GATE.** Code candor analyses soundly in ONE crate loses the effect entirely when split across a crate boundary with the dep report chained: `format!("{}", e)` on a dep type whose `Display::fmt` reads the clock gives `describe -> ['Clock']` combined and **PURE** chained — and `deny Clock` **exits 1 combined, 0 chained** | five root causes ranked: the join fires only on crate-QUALIFIED paths (desugared `Display::fmt`/`Drop::drop` emit none, though the dep report holds the right entry); no return types travel, so a factory-bound receiver is untyped; `interfaceUnion` is shipped-but-OFF in the default `--deps` path; `trait_decls` is local-only; coverage is crate-granular so one resolved call clears the blind flag for every shape. Also: the sweep's first framing ("chaining deletes the disclosure") was CORRECTED — the miss exists with and without chaining. **GATE-LEVEL IN ALL FOUR** (java 13/13 shapes, swift 7/13, ts 5 families, rust 5 — `deny` flips exit 1 → 0 in every one; implicit stringification is silent across the boundary in all four, the vein closed FOUR-WAY that same morning). **Each engine recovers something the others miss** (ts: inheritance + factory receivers + dep's Unknown; swift: dep's Unknown WITH whyReason + `CANDOR_WORKSPACE_CHAIN` on child scans; java: the direct-hash path), so the union of what they already do is close to the full fix — tractable, not fundamental. Originally stated as **GATE-LEVEL IN BOTH** (`deny Env` on the JVM fixture: exit 1 together, exit 0 chained). **JVM: 13 of 13 probed shapes read PURE (none Unknown)** — incl. the implicit-stringification vein CLOSED FOUR-WAY THE SAME MORNING, still live across the boundary (`app.S.show` Env→pure while the dep report holds `lib/Entry.toString…`→Env). JVM causes: CHA is project-only and an empty CHA emits no Unknown; `this.load()` on a dep supertype has a PROJECT owner so the join is never reached; HOF/callback surfaces project-only with the Unknown fallback gated off for exactly those cases; **a dep's own disclosed Unknown is dropped**. Information already present in 12/13. SOUNDNESS-VEIN-crossing-the-scan-boundary.md |
| global/lazy unit identity is not module-qualified | 2026-07-25 | **CLOSED four-way — rust `5447eba`, swift `b616caf`+`7cec437`+`7f18c38`** | **BOTH HALVES OF THE HONESTY-CRITICAL UNION FROM ONE DEFECT.** Two modules declaring a same-named module-scope global collapse into ONE unit with the union of their effects. **rust = SILENT UNDER-REPORT**: two `<lazy>::CFG` entries, same conflated loc, and the readers vanish (`util_uses`/`main` go `['Env']` → ABSENT/pure). **swift = FABRICATION**: `utilUses` is charged `Fs` from a global in another module it never touches | four-way sweep: **java SOUND** (`core.C.<clinit>`, fully qualified) and **ts SOUND** (`core.core.<module>`, path-qualified) — so the fix has in-family precedent, it is two engines not doing what the other two do. rust qualifies ordinary fns correctly (`util_m::util_uses`); the `<lazy>::` synthetic REPLACES the module path. Edge-filtering rejected (loses the genuine effect too). **rust FIXED**: `<lazy>::CFG` → `<lazy>::util_m::CFG`, module path INSIDE the prefix so tail2 stays discriminating; shared `lazy_qual` for declaration + forcing edge; A/B 0 gains / 0 losses on 8 codebases (a NO-REGRESSION result — none has an effectful module-scope lazy static — recovery pinned by the regression test); 178 tests, conformance 26/0. Spec-visible; blocker (a) of the initializer-edge vein. **SWIFT'S SIBLING IS IN FUNCTIONS TOO**: two modules' same-named `func shared()` are grouped as OVERLOADS and every caller edges to BOTH (`coreEntry`/`utilEntry` each get `['Env','Fs']`) — units stay distinct (`shared()`/`shared()#1`), RESOLUTION unions them. **"Prefer the caller's module" is UNSOUND, measured twice + reverted**: all-candidates dropped 19 real `Unknown`s on swift-syntax (a type extended in another module is idiomatic); narrowed to free functions it still dropped one — `SwiftSyntaxMacrosTestSupport` delegates to `SwiftSyntaxMacrosGenericTestSupport.assertMacroExpansion`, cross-module delegation between same-named free fns. **That lead became a SEPARATE CARDINAL SIN, now FIXED (`7cec437`)**: a module-qualified free call (`Core.shared()`) is neither `typed` nor `unqualified`, matched NO resolution branch, and produced NO edge — `delegates()` read sound-complete pure while calling a filesystem reader. The qualifier is NOT lost (the collector keeps it as `extOwner`; only `call.path` is stripped), so the fix is exact: base must be a real target, not also a local type, with exactly one free fn of that name. A/B 0 losses + **1 real recovery** (`assertMacroExpansion#1` gains `Unknown` on swift-syntax). **The UNQUALIFIED collision is ALSO FIXED (`7f18c38`)**: with qualified calls resolving on their own path, the twice-reverted module preference re-measured **clean (0 gains, 0 losses)** — the counterexample that killed it is written module-qualified, so it no longer had to steal that edge. The earlier revert was right about the measurement and wrong about the cause: it was judged on a broken foundation. Free functions only (members/inits legitimately span modules — 19 lost `Unknown`s when applied there). **SWIFT GLOBAL HALF ALSO FIXED (`b616caf`)**: file-scope globals are ACCESSOR units, which the overload disambiguation pass excludes — so two modules' `let cfg` merged. They now get the same `#n` treatment + module-scoped read resolution. A/B 0/0 on both corpora (no-regression; recovery pinned by test). **Vein CLOSED in all four engines**, and it clears blocker (a) of the initializer-edge vein. SOUNDNESS-VEIN-global-unit-identity.md |
| a caller of a BODY-LESS LOCAL DECLARATION read pure | 2026-08-13 | **CLOSED — candor-ts; pinned four-way by conformance PART 46** | **CARDINAL SIN, ts only.** `localName` mints a unit for any declaration it can name without asking whether it has a BODY, the call site edges the caller to that EMPTY unit, and the caller unioned nothing and was certified pure — a purity claim over code the engine never saw. `deny Unknown`, the gate whose entire purpose is "fail if candor cannot see what this reaches", exited **0** where rust/java/swift all exit 1. Engines legitimately differ on WHERE the charge lands (java on the DECLARATION unit, rust/swift at the EDGE), so PART 46 asserts on the CALLER's transitive set — what a gate reads — never the reason string. ts takes java's shape (mint-side = ONE place; edge-side = every call and desugar site, the drift that made `discloseUnanswerableKey` one function after being two). **THE OVER-CHARGE CONTROL IS MOST OF THE WORK, and it is measured**: charge only where NO LOCAL BODY ANSWERS, since a base member with a local bodied override is already resolved by the class-CHA. With that condition **zod +0** and **hono +18 → +9**; without it the crude rule charges hono's `EventProcessor` (6 abstracts, 3 local subclasses) and zod's `ZodType._parse`. All nine hono survivors are true positives (`Deno.mkdir`/`writeFile` Fs; `Deno.upgradeWebSocket`/`FetcherLike.fetch` Net — local `.d.ts` shims). Second trap: an OVERLOAD SET is N body-less signatures preceding the implementation under ONE unit name, so the marker mirrors `fns.set`'s last-write-wins or every overloaded function in every project becomes unanalysable. Both are fixtures; PART 46 carries the locally-bodied control in all four arms. **Calibrated** — re-run with the fix reverted, PART 46 reddens on ts alone. **RESIDUAL (open, filed in `candor/BACKLOG.md`)**: this closes the "candor cannot see" channel, not the blindness — axios `deny Unknown` is exit 1 now, **`deny Net` is still exit 0**, because the effects live in the 61 `.js` files the scan never reads |
| `Exec` charged the LAUNCH, not the subprocess CAPABILITY | 2026-08-24 | **CLOSED — candor-java; pinned four-way by conformance PART 66** | **SILENT UNDER-REPORT, java only.** The engine enumerated the launch verbs (`ProcessBuilder.start`/`startPipeline`, `Runtime.exec`, the `Desktop` openers) and the live-child control surface; everything else on `java.lang.ProcessBuilder` read pure. `ProcessBuilder arm(String[] argv) { return new ProcessBuilder(argv); }` reported `inferred: []` and passed `deny Exec` at **exit 0** — a fully-armed invocation assembled from caller-supplied argv, certified clean, because splitting build from launch across two methods (or two jars — the supply-chain form) left nobody holding the effect. Not a wrong rule, an ABSENT one: **an allowlist under-reports every verb nobody enumerated**. The type was already recognised at CONSTRUCTION for `cmds` extraction and even carried `incomplete: ["Exec"]` — recognised for the LITERAL, not for the EFFECT. rust/swift/ts already charged the capability; java was the lone outlier. | **Fixed as a DENYLIST** (SPEC §1 ⟨0.32⟩ requires that shape and forbids the allowlist): whole type is `Exec`, carve-outs by name — `environment()` stays `Env`, the NO-ARG read-back overloads (`command()`/`directory()`/`redirect*()`) keyed on the DESCRIPTOR because each shares its name with the setter (a blanket "no args ⇒ pure" would have exempted `start()` and `inheritIO()`), plus the §4 Object protocol. **Over-charge controls written FIRST and green before AND after**: read-back-only, a project-local type sharing the name, `environment()` staying `Env`, and an `OpenOptions`-shaped option builder for another effect staying pure (the ruling's boundary). **A/B: 933 JVM jars, 0 verdict flips / 0 effects lost / 0 functions newly flagged**, and the null EXPLAINED by a bytecode census — 21 `ProcessBuilder` call sites in 9 methods, all 9 launching in the same body — with the harness first proved to catch (rc 0→1 on the gap fixture). uflexi baseline byte-identical. **Cross-engine residuals found while building PART 66 and FILED, not fixed:** swift charges construction but not configuration and misses the qualified `Foundation.Process()` spelling; rust over-charges `get_program()` and an `OpenOptions` builder while missing `open` on a received one, and fabricates on a local `Command` when the file also imports the std one. | a peek finding is scope-matched against the WRONG ENTITY (the ⟨0.34⟩ peek-scope-match sin) | 2026-08-29 | **CLOSED four-way — candor-swift `7378f4f`, candor-rust `27f4beb`, candor-java `a034371`, candor-ts `8584572`; pinned by conformance PART 85 (falsified against each pre-fix commit)** | **SILENT UNDER-REPORT, all four engines, three different root causes.** A peek re-analyses excluded material and attributes a finding to the EXCLUDED declaration's own qualified name — correct — but the `<scope>` test a `deny`/`pure` rule runs was matched ONLY against that name, never against any IN-SCOPE CALLER reaching it via dynamic dispatch. Measured identically four ways: an in-scope dispatcher whose one visible implementer is pure, plus an excluded conformer/override performing `Net` reached only through that dispatcher — `deny Net Runner` answered `policy ✓`, `outOfScope: []`, exit 0, while the unscoped `deny Net` on the same tree already named the excluded declaration directly. swift's cause was CHA resolving over the peek's own in-scope/excluded union (a fix from an earlier cardinal sin, `9496d73`, had preserved attribution by skipping any finding whose function lived in a context file — false under dynamic dispatch, since the union can grow a context function's effect set past what the primary ever computed); rust and java never re-analyse in-scope code at all and simply never asked the caller's name; ts's dispatch mechanism was irrelevant (structural and nominal conformance failed identically), the tell that the defect was in WHICH ENTITY was asked, not in dispatch resolution. | **Fixed with four different mechanisms, one property** (SPEC §2/§6.2 ⟨0.34⟩): swift diffs the peek child's CHA-union effect set against the primary's own finalized result; rust cross-references dispatch SITES the primary already computes against the peek's excluded-type membership, with no re-analysis; java re-runs its own `runScan` over a scratch union rather than a second dispatch resolver; ts correlates a syntactic `satisfies`/`implements` annotation against interfaces the primary's own CHA already dispatches into. Where the responsible excluded declaration cannot be named with confidence, swift/java/ts disclose under a new `outOfScope[].class` value, `dispatch-widened`, rather than dropping the finding; **rust does not need it and must not be required to emit it**, since its peek attribution is never ambiguous by construction — mandating the class four-way would be dead vocabulary in one engine. PART 85's over-charge control asserts the fallback does NOT fire when attribution is unambiguous (all four engines), and a genuine firing case is proven positively on ts (an unresolvable `paths`-mapped interface reference). **Falsified against each engine's own pre-fix commit** in throwaway worktrees: rust (`27f4beb^`), java (`a034371^`, with a classpath-fix interaction isolated by using a flat compiled-output layout rather than a nested one), and swift (`7378f4f^`) all reproduce the exact silent exit-0 shape; ts required `npm install` in the pre-fix worktree to run at all, then reproduced identically. |
| rust-deep: closure/coroutine captures and `drop(x)` container walking were silently pure | 2026-08-29 | **CLOSED — candor-rust `3e9848c`** | **SILENT UNDER-REPORT, rust-deep only.** `mir_spike::local_drop_impls` (the implicit-Drop model backing rust's Drop-glue soundness) had two independent gaps: (1) a closure or coroutine that captured an effectful-`Drop` value BY MOVE, then was itself dropped without ever being called, read pure — `Closure`/`Coroutine`/`CoroutineClosure` are their own `TyKind`, not `TyKind::Adt`, so they fell into the walker's catch-all instead of being recursed into; (2) `mem_drop_local_edge` (the edge for an explicit `drop(x)`, which moves `x` into `mem::drop`'s non-local body) only ever resolved `<T as Drop>::drop` DIRECTLY on `T`, so `drop(Wrapper { g: Guard })` (no `impl Drop` on `Wrapper` itself, only a `Drop`-carrying field), `drop(Box::new(guard))`, and `drop(vec![guard])` all read pure, while the IDENTICAL value falling out of scope naturally was already caught — the same effect, two different silent answers depending on which of two hand-rolled paths asked. | Fixed by (1) recursing into `upvar_tys()` exactly as existing arms already recurse into fields/elements, and (2) routing `mem_drop_local_edge` through the SAME `local_drop_impls` walker instead of a narrower direct-only lookup — one shared implementation rather than two that could disagree, matching this session's recurring lesson (rust/java/swift's peek fixes above all reused an existing authority rather than hand-rolling a second one). |
| swift: R33 deinit-glue only fired for the unannotated `let`/`var` binder shape | 2026-08-29 | **CLOSED — candor-swift `10dc79e`** | **SILENT UNDER-REPORT, swift only, narrowing R33 (2026-07-18).** R33 charges an effectful `deinit` to the enclosing scope when a `let`/`var` local bound to a fresh construction goes out of scope (swift's sibling of rust Drop-glue). The mechanism lived ONLY in the UNANNOTATED arm of `visit(VariableDeclSyntax)`'s per-binding if/else-if, so the ordinary way to spell an explicitly-typed or Optional local — `let x: Loud = Loud(p)`, `var x: Loud? = Loud(p)` — took the OTHER arm and never reached it: the deinit effect vanished with NO disclosure at all. The wildcard binder (`let _ = Loud(p)`) fell through the identifier-only guard before either arm and missed it the same way. | Extracted the glue into one function, `applyDeinitGlue`, keyed off `rootOf` on the INITIALIZER expression (never the annotation, which is what let the annotated arm skip it before), called from both the annotated-binder arm and a new wildcard-binder check alongside the pre-existing unannotated call site — one shared implementation, the same shape as the rust fix above, so the annotated/unannotated/wildcard binder shapes cannot drift apart again. |
| java records: a component's effectful `equals`/`hashCode`/`toString` override ran unattributed | 2026-08-29 | **CLOSED — candor-java `3a84522`; falsified on 388 real third-party jars** | **CARDINAL SIN.** A record's generated `equals`/`hashCode`/`toString` is a single `invokedynamic` into `java.lang.runtime.ObjectMethods` (JEP 384) — the per-component comparison/combine/format runs inside the JDK bootstrap, never in bytecode the record's own method owns. `handleInvokeDynamic`'s `ObjectMethods` branch correctly skipped the bootstrap's per-component `H_GETFIELD` handles (field descriptors, not call targets, and feeding one to `methodId` would crash the scan) but never reentered the component's own contract method either — so a record component whose declared type overrides `equals`/`hashCode`/`toString` effectfully ran that override completely silent-pure, all the way up to a bare `main()`. Same shape as `StringConcatFactory`'s implicit `toString()` reentry (`"x" + obj`), already closed; left open for records specifically until now. | **Over-charge control, the strongest form used in this session:** candor-java's own output plus **388 real third-party jars** from local Maven/Gradle caches are byte-identical pre/post except ONE (`error_prone_core-2.50.0.jar`), where all 113 newly-surfaced record-contract methods land on `inferred: []` with an honest `invisible: ["com.google.common.collect"]` disclosure — never a fabricated effect. (A second apparent diff was reproduced away as I/O contention from the 388-jar batch; the real one was independently reproduced twice.) `soundness/run_kotlin.sh` and `kappa_libs_probe.py` were NOT run (kotlinc/JDK 21 absent) — pre-existing environment gaps, not introduced by this fix. |
| candor-agents: `deny Unknown` compiled to NOTHING, and `deny Net Unknown` silently dropped `Net` too | 2026-08-29 | **CLOSED — candor-agents `69e9e98`; first time this repo has been attacked** | **CARDINAL SIN in the ENFORCEMENT COMPILER, not the analyzer.** `guard.py` hand-rolls its own positional deny-parser instead of reusing the authoritative one in `policy.py`, so it never inherited `policy.py`'s `Unknown` special case: `guard.compile_guard("deny Unknown")` produced `{'deny': [], 'warnings': [], 'notes': []}` — total silence, the identical output an EMPTY policy produces — and `Unknown` names every uncurated MCP server and unresolved capability, so `deny Unknown` is the rule most likely to be load-bearing on the enforcement surface. In the compound form `deny Net Unknown`, `Unknown` was misread as a fictitious agent SCOPE, taking the genuine fleet-wide `deny Net` down with it. | Fixed by recognising `Unknown`/`Unknown[<class>]` as its own token class in `guard.py`; since guard genuinely cannot bind `Unknown` to a `permissions.deny` entry (there is no tool-level enforcement surface for "any unresolved capability"), it now emits an explicit warning rather than going silent. **Notable clean negative measured in the same session:** the rest of candor-agents (`drift`/`observe`/`scan`/`policy`) is heavily hardened, nearly every guard carrying a comment naming the real defect it closed, and every mutation tried against them was already caught — the break was isolated to the one component that reimplemented a shared parser instead of calling it. `stats.py`, `digest.py`, `savings.py`, `log_gate.py`, `agentsmd.py`, and several `scan.py` surfaces (`build_edges`, `classify_units`, cron-unit handling, hook-matcher edging, `--link`'s combined path) were read but explicitly NOT attacked — stated boundary, not a clean bill. |
| the consumer-refusal class: every `integrations/` consumer converted an engine's fail-closed refusal into a PASS | 2026-08-29 | **CLOSED — candor `0d483a8`, changelog/fixture correction `ac4a71b`; first time `integrations/` has been attacked as its own surface** | **THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL CLASS FOUND THIS SESSION** — not a scan-side blindness but a CONSUMER-side one, and it defeats every scan-side hardening rung at once, because ⟨0.21⟩ `unanalyzed`, ⟨0.24⟩ `refused`, ⟨0.28⟩'s armed sinks and ⟨0.30⟩ `incomplete` all exist to make a refusal MACHINE-VISIBLE, and three consumers threw that visibility away. (1) `integrations/claude-code/candor-review.sh`: the Stop-hook scripts collapsed "engine CRASHED" and "engine ran fine but REFUSED to certify because part of the target was unread" into one `rc=2`, and `stop-hook.sh` ALLOWS `rc=2` ("don't block on a misconfig") — reproduced end-to-end, a report carrying `unanalyzed`/`incomplete:true` at exit 2 produced *"review couldn't run (setup, rc=2)… not blocking."* A violation could sit in exactly the part the engine admits it never read. (2) `integrations/github/candor-sarif`: a REFUSED gate document (`ok:false, refused:true`, no `violations` key at all) and an INCOMPLETE one both produced a byte-identical, fully clean empty-results SARIF — a reviewer reading the persistent GitHub Security tab sees an all-clear. (3) `fingerprint/`: an `incomplete`/`unanalyzed` report still produced a maximally confident badge (`structure: 1`, "100% order") with nothing noting a whole module was never read. | Fixed by reading the completeness wire keys at each consumer: the Stop hook now returns `rc=1` (block) on a completeness failure, distinct from a genuine crash (still `rc=2`, proven by an over-charge control); `candor-sarif` uses SARIF's own `runs[].invocations` "tool did not fully succeed" channel (validated against the vendored 2.1.0 schema), leaving `results` untouched so a genuinely clean gate gets no `invocations` key; `fingerprint/` surfaces the gap in `--json`, on stderr, and in the HTML wrapper, with the deterministic SVG staying byte-identical. **THE LESSON, stated for reuse:** fail-closed is a property of a PIPELINE, not an engine — for any new disclosure key, ask what each consumer does when it is present, and specifically whether "the engine declined to answer" is distinguishable from "the engine answered clean". **Owed and explicitly unchecked (filed to candor `BACKLOG.md`, not this document):** `dispatch-widened` (the row above) landed in three engines the same day and no consumer has been checked against it; the `excluded`/`peeked`/`outOfScope`/`judgedElsewhere` family beyond `incomplete`/`unanalyzed`/`judgedNothing`/`refused`; `integrations/vscode` and `integrations/jetbrains` entirely. |
| THE INSTRUMENT SURVEY: 13 of 13 standalone conformance checkers could not fail | 2026-08-29 | **PARTIALLY FIXED — candor-spec `90cee30`; 7 of 13 hardened, 6 confirmed defeatable and filed** | **THE THIRD "instrument that cannot fail" found this session** (after `mutation-gate.sh`'s own blindness to standalone checkers, and java's `mutation_probe.sh` going a quarter blind silently — see the round above and SOUNDNESS-LOG.md). `mutation-gate.sh` extracts checker BODIES out of `run.sh`; a standalone `conformance/*.py` file is invisible to it by construction.
