the test is a REGRESSION pin for the twelve fields already there, not a COMPLETENESS pin, and it is written as though it were the latter.** | Not yet fixed. **Separately, TASK 3's enumeration: the window is COMPLETE with respect to `visit_local`'s own writes** — `BoundNameState` covers all 12 tables `visit_local` writes, and the other 4 name-keyed tables are never written there (`bound_names` is body-wide and computed once, so pre/post are identical by construction; its source `decls::pat_bound_idents` is a full `visit_pat_ident` walk, so no binder shape escapes it). **But it is INCOMPLETE with respect to reads INSIDE `visit_local`: five reads of name-keyed state are not wrapped in `with_pre_bindings` and run after writes in the same statement** — `tuple_of` (2932), `tuple_trait_of` (2943), `tuple_elem_leaves` inside the binding loop (2988, after the loop's own removals for earlier positions), `expr_is_fn_typed` (3081, after 3019/3034/3045/3068), and the `single_local` check (3141, after `closure_vars.remove` at 3034). Seven other reads ARE wrapped and correct. **And the `fn_alias` self-shadow at 3162 is handled by an explicit `shadowed_alias` local rather than the window — a second mechanism answering the same ordering question, which is precisely what this commit's own doc says removing the per-binder deferrals was meant to avoid.** Exploitation: the closure self-shadow through 3034/3141 IS a confirmed fabrication (`inferred: ["Env","Fs"]` where truth is `Fs`, from a free fn never called) but is **PRE-EXISTING, identical at `9c4d5be` and HEAD** — a standing over-charge, not this round's. The tuple-swap read at 2988 could not be isolated: the control arm loses dispatch typing too, so the instrument cannot separate the hypotheses (§E3) — reported unproven rather than claimed. **TWO OF THE FIVE READS I NAMED HAVE NO DIFFERENTIAL, and the agent said so instead of claiming five fixes.** `tuple_of` (2932), `tuple_trait_of` (2943) and `expr_is_fn_typed` (3081) do NOT run after a write to the tables they read — nothing above them writes those. They were wrapped anyway and **labelled as having no differential**. The three that are real: **`tuple_elem_leaves` (2988) was a SILENT UNDER-REPORT** — `let (d, n) = (d, n); d.go()` absent from `functions[]` while the non-shadowing twin `let (e, m) = (d, n); e.go()` reports `['Fs']`; `single_local` (3141) and the closure-rebind marking are fabrications, both **PRE-EXISTING at `9c4d5be`**; and `fn_alias`'s `shadowed_alias` local — the second mechanism answering the ordering question the window exists to own — is **deleted**, not left beside it. **THE COMPLETENESS CLAIM WAS RE-PROVEN FALSE INDEPENDENTLY** rather than taken from the audit: a 13th name-keyed table added and poisoned inside the window's scope, and the pin passed with all 390 tests. The compiler forces the field into ten exhaustive struct literals, **none of which is `BoundNameState`/`capture_bindings`/`restore_bindings`.** The comment is now worded as the regression pin it actually is. **OPEN RESIDUAL, and it is the honest one:** a name-keyed table added to `CallCollector` and not registered in `BoundNameState` is outside the window and **nothing in the repo will say so.** Making it genuinely complete needs the field list derived from one place — not done. |

## ⚠ OWED TO TOM — a gate-semantics question raised by R105's fix (2026-09-02)

R105 replaced "pick the `#[cfg]` arm that happens to be last in the file" with an honest
`Unknown` + `ambiguous:` when the arms classify differently. That is unambiguously more truthful. **But it
changes what a bare `deny <E>` does, and the change deserves a ruling rather than a default.**

Measured on two crates identical but for arm order, one arm `Fs` and one `Env`:

| policy | pre-fix (arm A) | pre-fix (arm B) | post-fix (both) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `deny Fs` | 1 | 0 | **0** |
| `deny Unknown` | 0 | 0 | **1** |
| `deny Fs Unknown` | 0 | 0 | **1** |

Post-fix the two orders agree under every form — that is the point, and it is right. The question is the
first row. **Pre-fix, `deny Fs` fired for roughly half the affected crates by coin-flip. Post-fix it never
fires**, because the honest answer is `Unknown` and a bare `deny <E>` does not act on `Unknown`.

**The engine now KNOWS `Fs` is one of exactly two possibilities and does not fail closed on it.** That is
defensible — `deny Unknown` is the documented upgrade and it does fire — but it is a different posture
from the rest of the family, where an effect the engine can name is denied. The alternative is: **a
`deny <E>` fires when `<E>` appears among the arms of an `ambiguous:` disclosure.**

This is a **cross-engine SPEC question**, not a rust one — java/ts/swift have not been measured for the
same shape. No engine or spec text was changed. **Tom's call.**
| R108 rust: an eval gate that could not tell "no finding" from "no run" — and its ABSENCE CONTROL printed PASS over a dead instrument | 2026-09-02 | **CLOSED — candor-rust `75b57ca`. A HARNESS defect. MY DIAGNOSIS IN THE ORIGINAL ROW WAS WRONG AND INVERTED** | **NEITHER FAILURE WAS A DETECTION DEFECT. `candor` never ran.** I filed this as two silent under-reports in the architecture gate. Measured: `candor-scan` and the dylint evaluator record both cross-crate edges and act on them correctly — re-running with a loadable host-ABI library gives `[AS-EFF-008] billing::record_activity reaches { metrics.growthtracker.io:443 } outside the allowlist` and `[AS-EFF-009] domain::checkout reaches into a forbidden layer (via infra)`, with `charge_customer` and `subtotal` correctly absent, ground-truthed against the fixture crates' source. **AND MY KEY INFERENCE WAS BACKWARDS.** I wrote that the passing step-3 assertion proved *"the harness is live and the DETECTION is what fails"*. It proves the opposite: `cargo candor policy` uses a DIFFERENT and correct locator (`newest_of`, `cargo-candor:57-73`, whose own comment reads *"Explicit mtime scan, NOT `ls … \| head`"*), so step 3 passing is evidence the ENGINE works, while steps 1–2 never loaded it. **Root cause, one copy-pasted line in both scripts, unchanged since `c572e92`:** the library is selected by `find … -name '*.dylib' -o -name '*.so' \| head -1` — platform-blind and ORDER-based — and guarded by `[ -e "$LIB" ]`. Two defects, both failing in the miss direction: a foreign-ABI Linux `.so` in the shared `target/debug` is listed first and handed to `cargo dylint`, which refuses it; and on no match `basename ""` is empty, so `$LIB` becomes the DIRECTORY and `-e` accepts it — **so `"FAIL: no candor dylib"` has never once been reachable.** The `origin/main` worktree I measured hit the second defect (never built); the local clone hit the first. **AND I CAUSED THE LOCAL ONE:** my Docker oracle run at 00:53:56 dropped a Linux `.so` into the shared `target/debug`, because `soundness/realworld/run.sh` honours `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` for the build but hardcodes `$ROOT/target/debug` for the scanner — **a hazard the R102 agent had reported to me hours earlier and I did not connect.** CI has never seen either defect: ubuntu builds only a `.so` (no ambiguity) and always builds first (no guard hole); `gh run list` shows **`ci success 3cf055d`**, the very commit I measured red. | **THE FINDING THAT OUTLIVES THE BUG, and it is why my confidence was misplaced rather than merely wrong: each script pairs a positive assertion with an ABSENCE CONTROL — and absence is exactly what a DEAD INSTRUMENT PRODUCES.** So the dead run printed `FAIL` on the positive and `PASS` on the control, and that pairing is precisely what made me read it as a precise, trustworthy detection failure. **A control that asserts nothing-is-there cannot distinguish a working engine from an absent one unless something proves the stream was written** — §E3 one level up from "the fixture must compile and run". Fixed with a single locator/liveness primitive (`eval/bet3/_lib.sh`: newest HOST-ABI library by mtime, a guard that fails on empty rather than on `-e` of a reconstructed path, and `assert_live`, which refuses to adjudicate a stream candor did not write — keyed on cargo's INDENTED `Checking`/`Finished`, since dylint's unindented preamble prints even when nothing loads). **`verify-instrument.sh` makes the calibration a GATE**: three dead-instrument states plus a green control, asserting each script exits non-zero, names the reason, and **credits no `PASS`** — falsified against the pre-fix scripts, where it fails 5/5 and reproduces the vacuous `PASS: pure domain::subtotal not flagged` verbatim. All four teeth scripts now rc=0 **with the offending `.so` still present**. **The same locator class survives elsewhere in candor-rust, unfixed and listed:** `integrations/claude-code/test-receipt.sh:59` (degrades to a skip), `install.sh:52`, `tests/integration.sh:20`, `soundness/{run,run_cross,run_drop}.sh` (mtime-correct but platform-blind), and **the recipe published to downstream agents in `AGENTS.md:92` and its two copies under `crates/`.** Read-only cross-engine sweep: the full combination is unique to candor-rust, with two live single-defect instances elsewhere — `candor-spec/.github/workflows/conformance.yml:257` and `candor-swift/soundness/confirmatory/run_frozen.sh:74` — both retaining working guards, neither touched. |
| R109 ts: `localStorage` fails closed ONLY when `lib.dom` is absent — the spelling every browser project uses is silent | 2026-09-02 | **CLOSED — candor-ts `e620af4`. Was PRE-EXISTING and present in published artifacts, in the DEFAULT configuration** | **CARDINAL SIN, ts, and a DOCUMENTED LIMITATION THAT IS FALSE FOR THE COMMON CASE.** `scan-core.mjs:377` states of `web-globals/storage` that *"it is not inert and has not been modelled — it fails closed."* **That is true for the `@types/node` spelling and false for the `lib.dom` one.** With `"lib": ["ES2022","DOM"]` — what every browser and isomorphic project configures — `localStorage.setItem("token", secret)` resolves to lib.dom's `Storage` interface, lands on the es-lib "conventionally-pure" arm, and the report is **EMPTY**: coordinator-verified `functions: []`, `deny Unknown` **exit 0**, over a call that writes caller-supplied secret material to persistent storage. Under `@types/node` the same call correctly yields `Unknown[native:web-globals/storage.getItem]` and `deny Unknown` exits 1 — **so the engine has a right answer and a wrong one for the same source line, selected by tsconfig.** This is [[feedback-documented-limitation-is-not-measured]] exactly: the comment reads as considered, and the consideration covered one spelling. Found outside its brief's scope by the R95 agent, which reported it rather than folding it into a fabrication-sensitive fix. | Not yet fixed. **The fix shape is already in the file, two lines above the false comment** — the `crypto` note handles precisely this es-lib/`@types/node` split, and the same shape applies here: an es-lib arm keyed `parent === "Storage"`. **Needs a four-way parity decision, which is why it was left:** `localStorage` is a browser surface, so java has no analogue, but swift (`UserDefaults`) and the ts/rust WASM paths may. **Also worth noting the R95 agent self-caught an instrument error while finding this** — it nearly filed a `deny Unknown` false-pass that was its own `$?`-after-a-same-line-command-substitution bug, re-measured, and reported the real finding instead. That is the error I have made twice in this session and warned every brief about. **MY ROW WAS WRONG IN ONE PLACE AND UNDERSTATED IN ANOTHER, and the understatement is the important one.** Wrong: the two arms I described were the SAME arm — a tsconfig with `types:["node"]` and no explicit `lib` **still resolves `localStorage` through lib.dom**, so both of my configurations gave `functions: []`. The `@types/node` spelling is reached only by a tsconfig naming `lib` WITHOUT `DOM`. Understated: **lib.dom is the DEFAULT `lib` for any ES target — so the silently-pure half was the DEFAULT configuration and the correct half was the narrow one**, the reverse of how I filed it. (Also `setItem` yields `native:…storage.setItem`, not `.getItem`.) **FIXED with one es-lib branch keyed on the resolved declaration's PARENT (`Storage`), not on identifier text** — converging on the `@types/node` arm's answer, covering the whole interface rather than a verb list. `sessionStorage` falls out of the same predicate, measured not assumed. Coordinator-verified under the default configuration: `stash` and `stashSession` both `['Unknown']` + `native:Storage.setItem`, `deny Unknown` 0 → 1, while `Math.random`/`Date.now` still correctly charge `Rand`/`Clock` — the precision control holding. **THE KEYING DECISION PAID OFF ON REAL CODE:** an identifier-text key would have reported all three of `this.sessionStore.getItem(k)` (@supabase/phoenix), `let storage = self.localStorage; storage.setItem(…)` (redux-persist) and a storage FACTORY's return value (jotai) as pure. A/B over **194 npm packages** with deps installed, keyed wide: **ADDED 5, REMOVED 0, CHANGED 41**, with **113 hits across 8 packages** — not safety-only — and **all 46 moved rows audited in full** (24 direct, 22 transitive proven by calls-closure after a first pass classified some on a name suffix and was re-run strictly), zero fabrications. Revert test: 30 of 41 checks red; the 11 invariant ones say so in their own text, **including the `@types/node` convergence control, which exists because "make both configs agree" is also satisfiable by breaking the correct one.** **The sweep was answered MECHANICALLY, not sampled:** over all 16 `@types/node/web-globals` files, `storage` is the ONLY member that fails closed in this direction. **Near-miss disclosed by the fixer:** `git checkout HEAD~1 -- scan.mjs` for the revert test staged the revert, and its first `--amend` committed a `scan.mjs` WITHOUT the fix while the message described it — **caught only because it printed `git status --porcelain` instead of trusting an echo**, then re-ran every gate from an independent worktree of the final commit. 1825 tests (from 1784). |

Two fixtures pin the current (wrong) answer so it cannot move by accident. **THE STANDING CONCLUSION FROM THE R109/R110/R111 TRIPLE, and it is worth more than any of the three: the two tables that answer \"is this web global effect-bearing?\" — `scan.mjs`'s es-lib `parent === X` arm and `scan-core.mjs`'s κ/floor keyed on `declModule` — have now disagreed in BOTH directions and AGREED while both were wrong. So agreement between the two resolution paths is worthless as a signal, and no future reasoning may treat either as the trusted one.** A costed reconciliation was proposed and deliberately NOT built: a single source of truth keyed on the resolved declaration's INTERFACE name regardless of declaring file, ~1 day, covering the es-lib arm's ~8 `parent ===` branches (`Storage`, `Crypto`, `Clipboard`, `Navigator`, `XMLHttpRequest`, `Performance`, `DateConstructor`, `Math`) whose node-side twins live in three different tables — **a partial unification, not a clean one, because the es-lib arm carries locator/`incomplete` logic (XHR argument 1, `sendBeacon` argument 0) that κ has no place for.** Also unswept: the remaining es-lib `parent ===` arms for the same twin-drift, and the OPFS/IndexedDB/`caches` surface R109 named. **MY FOUR-MEMBER LIST WAS THREE. `timerify` does NOT read the clock** — measured under node 22.12.0, it returns a NEW WRAPPER (`t !== fn`, two calls give two objects) and the read happens when the WRAPPER runs, observed as a `PerformanceObserver` entry with its own `startTime`. **Charging it would have fabricated**, and the hole it leaves (`const t = performance.timerify(f); t(1)` is silent) is a **factory-returning-an-effectful-callable** class, filed separately rather than folded in. Two further exclusions measured rather than assumed: `toJSON()` returns a **live reference** to `nodeTiming`, so the read is the later `.duration` access; and **`timeOrigin`/`nodeTiming`/`PerformanceEntry.startTime` are PROPERTY ACCESSES, which the classify site (`isCallExpression \|\| isNewExpression`) cannot see at all — no regex in either table can reach them.** That is a different fact from "judged pure" and is a lead, not a purity ruling. Coordinator-verified: `mark`/`measure`/`eventLoopUtilization` charge `Clock`; `timerify` and `clearMarks` correctly stay pure. **THE DEGREE OF FREEDOM BEHIND R109/R110/R111 IS GONE FOR THIS INTERFACE: the member set is now ONE exported constant read by BOTH tables, asserted by IDENTITY (`vre === CONST`) rather than by two regexes that happen to spell the same thing today.** This is NOT the reconciliation — the other ~8 `parent ===` arms still have separate node-side twins — but it sharpens that estimate: the shared-constant move cost ~20 minutes for one arm, so the ~1 day is mostly the arms carrying locator/`incomplete` logic, not plumbing. **THE OVER-CHARGE FEAR WAS ANSWERED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT ARGUMENT:** both 194-package corpora are byte-identical **and SAFETY-ONLY, recorded as such at the time** — grep on package source finds 0 call sites of the new members, with `performance.now(` in 11 files as the positive control. So a **371-package recall corpus** was built for User Timing: **0.28% and 0.21% of rows move, 3 packages, REMOVED 0**, all **268** moved rows audited in full (19 direct, 14 direct-but-already-`Clock` and visible only on the wide key, 104 transitive — every transitive one proven by closure over recorded `calls` edges, 0 unproven, 0 fabrications). **`eventLoopUtilization` had ZERO real call sites across all 565 packages — every match was JSDoc — so it got its own recall arm: `@fastify/under-pressure`'s `updateEventLoopUtilization` went from ABSENT (certified pure) to `['Clock']`, scoped `deny Clock` 0 → 1.** Revert: 22 of 53 red, 31 invariant by design — **and the revert test caught a gate of the agent's own that could not fail**, a lib.dom `deny Clock` check sitting on a tree that also contained `performance.now()`, so it exited 1 even with the fix reverted. No new mechanism is needed: these are call sites, so the existing κ rule reaches them, and R111 has already made the `Performance` member set one shared constant asserted by identity — **the same treatment is what stops this recurring a fourth time.** **FIXED, and the sweep past the trigger found THREE MORE that this row never named.** (1) **`process.hrtime()`'s tuple form** — my "no new machinery" was wrong: it is a CALL SIGNATURE on `HRTime`, so κ was asked about the empty token `""` and the floor reviewed that as pure; closed by a strictly-additive retry that re-asks with the property name when the first lookup answers nothing on an empty member, which cannot move a call off an existing effect. (2) **`process.hrtime.bigint()` was SILENT via a MODULE IMPORT** — my row called it correctly `Clock`, true only through the global. A live two-answer disagreement: `scan.mjs`'s global arm charged it while `scan-core.mjs`'s floor listed `hrtime`/`bigint` as reviewed-pure, **under a comment asserting purity *"for the reason `Date.now()` is pure in this engine: nothing charges Clock for reading a timer"* — false in BOTH halves.** (3) **`console.time`/`timeLog`/`timeEnd`**: executed, two timers started 80 ms apart end 80 ms apart — `console.time()` itself takes the timestamp, and SPEC §1's console exemption is about WRITES, not the monotonic read. **The exclusions were each executed and deliberately left, on a stated criterion — "moving is not the criterion; reading TIME is":** `os.freemem` (7912144896 → 7736311808 over 300 ms), `totalmem`, `loadavg`, `cpus()[].times`, `process.cpuUsage`/`resourceUsage`/`memoryUsage`/`constrainedMemory`/`availableMemory`. `os.platform`/`arch`/`EOL`/`machine`/`getPriority` and `process.pid` are the precision control in both arms. **R111's shape applied to the pair that had actually drifted:** `CLOCK_READING_PROCESS_MEMBERS` and `CLOCK_READING_CONSOLE_MEMBERS` are shared constants asserted by identity, and `uptime`/`hrtime`/`bigint` were **REMOVED from the floor list rather than left inert**. A/B isolated against the R115 image: 0/0/0 over 20,777 rows — **and the zero is EXPLAINED, not trusted: 18 counted branch hits, every one on a function already carrying `Clock` from another read in the same body, and the corpus has ZERO `uptime` call sites, so for that half it is safety-only and the commit says so.** |
| R115 ts: `{...instance}` charges a class PROTOTYPE getter that never runs — a fabrication that FAILS a gate | 2026-09-02 | **CLOSED — candor-ts `e5c60bc`. PRE-EXISTING; the OPPOSITE direction from R113/R114, at the same site** | **FABRICATION, ts, and the only finding of this vein that makes candor fail a gate on code that does nothing.** `enumerateGetters` (`scan.mjs:4305`) is called from `{...obj}`, `Object.assign(t, obj)` and `const {...rest} = obj`. **Its own comment states the correct rule** — *"copying an object's **own enumerable** props INVOKES each source getter"* — **and the body does not apply it**: `type.getProperties()` returns inherited prototype accessors, and a class accessor is on the prototype and non-enumerable, so spread never copies it. EXECUTED, counting invocations: `{...c}` → **0**, `Object.assign({},c)` → **0**, `JSON.stringify(c)` → 0, `Object.entries(c)` → 0, while `const {tok}=c` → 1 and `c.tok` → 1 and `{...objectLiteral}` → 1 (the case the mechanism was written for). Isolated, `tsc`-clean: `export function clone(s: Session) { return { ...s }; }` over a class whose getter reads `/etc/token` gives candor `['Fs']` and **`deny Fs` exit 1 — `[AS-EFF-006] src.a.clone performs { Fs }` — while the executed program shows `readFileSync_calls: 0`.** The `JSON.stringify`/`Object.entries` purity is CORRECT, not a gap; the mechanism is wrong only in the direction it does fire. | Not yet fixed. **Narrowing direction per [[candor-denylist-over-allowlist]]: exclude only accessors whose declaration parent is a `ClassDeclaration`/`ClassExpression`** — provably prototype — and leave interface and type-literal accessors charged, since the runtime object could be either. **PRICE HONESTLY UNMEASURED and said so:** the agent instrumented the branch, calibrated it (2/2 hits on its own fixture, both class-prototype), ran it over 6 real repos and got **zero hits** — so this corpus cannot reach the branch and real-world frequency is unknown; the finding is a recall-hunt construction. **Two smaller siblings reported at the same site:** `c[k]` with a dynamic key over a receiver whose type carries an effectful accessor reads PURE, while the `Object.defineProperty` analogue already takes the opposite posture (`Unknown[reflect:defineProperty:dynamic-key]`) — two implementations of one question, drifted (§F1-3); and an object-literal getter read hedges `Unknown[reflect:accessor:?]` rather than edging to a visible local unit — fail-closed, so a precision cost rather than a sin. **FIXED, and my proposed predicate was wrong: `find` over the get-declarations would have failed** — a union or mapped property symbol carries declarations from EVERY constituent, so it must be `every`. Measured: `Session \| { get token(): string }` keeps its charge only under `every`. **Three spellings this row never named were also fabricating and fall to the same predicate:** an inherited base-class getter, a class EXPRESSION, and a **static accessor on the constructor** (own but non-enumerable — descriptor printed, executed 0). **The narrowing opens a real hole and it was pinned rather than asserted away:** TypeScript is structural, so `const s: Session = { get token(){…} }; {...s}` DOES invoke the getter (executed 1). Where the source is a plain binding the literal's accessors are now enumerated directly; **where it arrives via a PARAMETER there is no initializer, and that residual is pinned by a fixture asserting today's WRONG answer** — pre-fix that spelling was charged only by coincidence, the class getter's effects standing in for the literal's. **THREE INSTRUMENTS HELD THE SAME WRONG PREMISE:** the implementation, a unit test (*"the copier inherits the precise Exec"*, now flipped with its fixture untouched), and **`fuzz.mjs`'s `obj_spread` GENERATOR**, which emitted a class and asserted the caller must not be omitted. **`node test.mjs` was green throughout — only the full `npm test` (fuzz) caught the third**, and the generator's teeth were re-verified by reintroducing the hole (seeds 14/15/22 go red). A/B over **25 real TypeScript-source repos** — the 371-package corpus I cited **does not exist**, and a `.d.ts`-only corpus cannot reach a branch that needs a spread in a real body — keyed wide: ADDED 0, **REMOVED 1**, CHANGED 6, 0 on `inferred`, **290 branch hits (got 272, nest 17, typeorm 1) so NOT safety-only.** The single removal, `got`'s `Options.toJSON`, ground-truthed **from source**: `#internals` is a mapped type carrying the class getters' declarations over a plain literal built by `cloneInternals` — **48 fabricated edges, and the function really is pure.** Revert: 11 red, 12 invariant by design, **and it caught a dead assertion of the agent's own** ("something was charged", which the pre-fix engine also satisfies; now asserts the reason). |

Sound direction proposed by the finder: for each `Object.assign` source, take the source type's property names and look up `set` accessors on the TARGET type; charge all target setters when a source type yields no resolvable properties. **R115's lesson applies directly to whoever takes this:** its predicate needed `every` rather than `find` over declarations because a union or mapped symbol carries declarations from every constituent, and three instruments — implementation, unit test, and the fuzz GENERATOR — all encoded the same wrong premise, with only the fuzz arm catching the third. |

Caught only because that result was not credible. This is the documented hard rule (never read `$?` after a pipe OR after a same-line command substitution) and I broke it twice in the session whose subject is instruments that cannot fail. **A second self-inflicted error, same verification:** my first fixture named the control `underReportUnannotated`, which is a PREFIX of the scope `underReport`, so `deny Fs underReport` matched the control and exited 1 — a scope-match artefact that would have masked the finding. The single changed row is `time` 0.3.55 regaining an `invisible: serde_core` disclosure, audited to source as the stated residual firing, `inferred` unchanged. Revert test RUN not reasoned: all three new tests fail on the pre-fix tree, each on its intended assertion. `cargo test --workspace` rc=0, 326+77. `bin/gate-run.sh candor-rust` 25 run / 25 ok; 10 UNRUN (5 provisioning SKIPs, 5 Linux+strace SELFSKIPs), reported as unrun. **COORDINATOR ERROR CORRECTED IN THE SAME BREATH:** the fixing commit and 12 in-tree references numbered this **R109**, which was already taken by the ts `localStorage` row. Caught because the pre-image binary was absent and the policy runs returned **exit 127**, which is indistinguishable from a violation if only the exit code is read. Build with `-p candor-scan`. |

Against the PUBLISHED binary, in a crate containing no portable-`fs` call anywhere: **`functions: 0`, `excluded: []`, and ALL FIVE policy forms exit 0** — `deny Fs`, `deny Unknown`, `deny Fs Unknown`, `pure make_link`, `deny Fs make_link`. At the fix: `functions: 2` and all four `Fs`-bearing forms exit 1. **The control is what makes it a one-variable result:** the same write through the PORTABLE module (`std::fs::copy`) reads `['Fs']` in both binaries, so the only variable is the module path. In a crate that also uses portable `fs`, blanket `deny Fs` exits 1 — but only INCIDENTALLY, via the other call. | **The rust half is fixed with a trait-keyed DENYLIST** ([[candor-denylist-over-allowlist]]): `MetadataExt`/`PermissionsExt`/`OpenOptionsExt` and friends stay pure, `FileExt` does not — **and those exemptions are TESTED, not asserted in a comment** (§E2). A/B: **ADDED 34, REMOVED 0, nothing lost.** **THE ROW STAYS OPEN BECAUSE THE QUESTION IS FOUR-WAY AND HAS NOT BEEN ASKED OF THE OTHER THREE ENGINES.** The generalisable question is not about rust: **does each engine's filesystem rule cover only the PORTABLE spelling, and what does it do with the platform-specific, POSIX or alternate-module route to the same syscall?** That is brief §F1 q3 (separate implementations that drift) crossed with §9 (do not draw the audit around its own trigger). Agents are dispatched for java, ts and swift; the rust fix is not evidence about any of them. **THE JAVA HALF CAME BACK MUCH BIGGER THAN THE QUESTION, AND THE QUESTION WAS TOO NARROW.** It is not that java's `Fs` rule covered one spelling: **THIRTEEN routes across FIVE effects** were silent, and they share one mechanism. Each was isolated in a class containing NO ordinary-spelling call, compiled and RUN so the real effect was observed — a real symlink, a real 0400 chmod, a real `user.candor` xattr, a real file trashed, 807 real OS process handles, a real child waited for, a real DNS lookup resolving `localhost` and failing on `.invalid` — then scanned: `functions: []`, `excluded: []`, **exit 0 under all five policy forms**, with the same-operation control charging and exiting 1 in the same jar. **Fs:** `java.nio.file.spi.FileSystemProvider` — **the SPI every `Files.*` method is DEFINED as a call to** — plus `FileSystem.getFileStores/getRootDirectories/newWatchService/close`, the `*AttributeView` family (chmod/chown/utimes/setxattr via a view received as a parameter), `UserPrincipalLookupService`, and `Desktop.moveToTrash`. **Exec:** `Process.onExit/inputReader/errorReader/outputWriter` and `ProcessHandle.onExit/allProcesses/children/descendants/parent/of`. **Rand:** the `RandomGenerator$Splittable/Jumpable/Streamable` sub-interfaces. **Net:** `new InetSocketAddress(String,int)`, `InetAddress.getHostName`, `Inet4/6Address` receivers, `SSLServerSocket`, `ServerSocketFactory.createServerSocket`, `JarURLConnection`. **Clock:** `GregorianCalendar.getInstance()`. **COORDINATOR-VERIFIED ON THE HEADLINE ROUTE, and java's failure is a STRONGER claim than rust's.** Isolated fixture, ground truth EXECUTED (`/tmp/candor_spi_link -> /etc/hosts` confirmed by `ls`): at the pre-image jar `2dd1600`, `app.Spi.viaProvider` is **PRESENT in `functions[]` with `inferred: []`** — not omitted, but **affirmatively listed as effect-free** — and all five policy forms exit 0. At `d8e953c` it is `['Fs']` and all four `Fs`-bearing forms exit 1. **In rust the unit VANISHED; in java it is reported as pure.** Same silence, but java's is a positive false statement rather than an absence, which matters for how a reader of the report is misled. **THE MECHANISM (the reusable part):** all thirteen are the classifier keying on the **static receiver type in the bytecode**, in one of three shapes — (a) a subtype-typed receiver of a modelled type, where the supertype re-classification at `Candor.handleMethodInsn` is gated on `ctx.byName.containsKey(min.owner)`, i.e. **PROJECT OWNERS ONLY, so no JDK subtype ever gets the walk**; (b) the layer BELOW a modelled facade; (c) a verb allowlist that forgot a member. Shape (a) had already been patched by hand three times (`MulticastSocket`, `SSLSocket`, Conscrypt) — **each boundary drawn around its own trigger**, which is §9 three times over. The agent widened past that by sweeping **13,324 public JDK types against the 172 owners the classifier models**; `SSLServerSocket`, `Inet4/6Address` and `JarURLConnection` came out of that sweep rather than out of anyone's list. Two §E2 hits: the `Rand` rule's comment asserted the sub-interfaces *"extend it"* (true of the type system, false of bytecode), and the `Process`/`ProcessHandle` rules were ALLOWLISTS in a file whose own `ProcessBuilder` rule argues at length that allowlists under-report silently. A/B over 395 third-party jars keyed on EVERY field: 577,547 common rows, **ADDED 107, REMOVED 0, zero losses on any field**. §E1 counters in all 14 changed branches: **11 reached** (clock.calendar 279, exec.process 111, net.sockaddr 90, fs.filesystem 61, fs.provider 57 …) — a RECALL measurement; three branches got zero corpus hits and are **safety-only over this corpus**, each resting on an executed fixture instead, and that is stated rather than blended in. Real code newly reported: httpcore5 `DefaultAddressResolver.resolve`, okhttp `Util.peerName`, jackson `InetSocketAddressSerializer`, smallrye `GetAllProcessesInfoAction`. Revert test done BY REVERTING: 6/6 positive tests go red; the 7 over-charge controls pass in both arms **and the agent explicitly does not claim they discriminate the fix**. **TWO ERRORS THE AUDIT CAUGHT IN ITSELF, both worth keeping:** its first cut FABRICATED `Fs` on `FileSystemProvider.getPath(URI)`/`getFileSystem(URI)` — four real rows in jetbrains verifier-cli — which the SPI specifies as non-I/O; carved out, pinned, A/B re-run. And `ServerSocketFactory.createServerSocket` first read as ALREADY COVERED because the fixture also called `ServerSocket.close()`, which is charged; re-run with the bind alone, both arms said `functions: []`. **A mixed fixture cannot answer an isolation question** — the same trap that made my own blanket-deny readings incidental three times today. **THE SWIFT HALF FOUND TWO SINS, AND THE FIRST IS THE MOST ELEGANT FAILURE OF THIS ROUND.** candor-swift already HAD a raw-syscall disclosure — R61's `native:<name>` — so `symlink` was never uncovered. **It was gated on the file importing `Darwin`/`Glibc`/`Musl`/`WinSDK`, and Foundation RE-EXPORTS the platform C library.** So the disclosure existed, was correct, and simply did not fire for the import almost every real Swift file uses. Coordinator-verified, ground truth EXECUTED in BOTH arms (each creates a real symlink, confirmed by `lstat` + `S_IFLNK` in the program's own output), isolated — no `FileManager` call anywhere — with **the import line as the only variable**: at the PUBLISHED baseline `819fac6`, `import Darwin` gives `doWork` -> `['Unknown']` + `unknownWhy: ['native:symlink']` and **`deny Unknown` exit 1**, while `import Foundation` gives **`functions: 0`, `doWork` ABSENT, and all five policy forms exit 0** — no `Unknown`, no `invisible`, no `incomplete`, a positive purity claim over an executed syscall, **for the whole R61 allowlist at once**. At HEAD both arms are identical. Fixed by removing the module gate entirely — the NAME allowlist is the only gate — plus one PROVABLE narrowing: a C function imported into Swift has no argument labels, so `remove(at: i)` cannot be libc's `remove`. **§9 half:** the original boundary had been drawn around R61's own repro, so the audit swept outward and found the `*at` twins of five listed names plus `openat`/`chroot`/`chdir`/`mkfifo`/`mknod`/`creat`/`opendir`/`readdir`/`flock`/`fsync`/`mmap`/`umask`/`waitpid`/`copyfile`/`sendfile`, the `execl*` family and the remaining `set*id` setters — **all silent under BOTH imports**. `execve`/`execvP`/`fexecve`/`posix_spawnp` were in NEITHER table and are now concretely `Exec` and ESTABLISHING, so a runtime command through them can no longer mask an `allow Exec` allowlist. **SECOND SIN (`29f317a`), and MY BRIEF'S GUESSES WERE WRONG:** I named `Data.write(to:)` and `String(contentsOfFile:)` as suspects and **both are modelled**. What was not: `NSString`/`NSDictionary`/`NSArray(contentsOfFile:)` — `NSDictionary(contentsOfFile:)` being how a decade of plist-reading Swift is written — in a classifying function whose own title is *"ONE FUNCTION, BOTH SPELLINGS"* and which knew three of the six type spellings; `NSData.write(toFile:)`, the bridged twin of the charged `Data.write(to:)`; **`URL` had no κ entry AT ALL** (`checkResourceIsReachable()` is a `stat` and the idiomatic Swift file-existence test, sibling of the always-`Fs` `FileManager.fileExists`), plus `resourceValues`/`setResourceValues`/`bookmarkData`/`resolvingSymlinksInPath`; and `OutputStream(toFileAtPath:)`/`InputStream(fileAtPath:)`, **inexpressible in `kappaFree` because it is keyed on `(name, argCount)` and only the LABEL separates a file open from `InputStream(data:)`**. A/B over 13 real packages, 11,008 common rows, wide key (13 fields): **ADDED 3, REMOVED 0, CHANGED 53**, all 56 audited against source with no sampling, branch counters confirming the corpus reaches the change (20 hits in 3 packages) — a RECALL measurement. Revert-tested by actually reverting at the parent commits: 7/9 and 5/5 rows red, the green ones named as controls. Gates all green, 1047/1047 on macOS AND under `swift:6.1` on Linux; the two macOS-SELFSKIP gates run under Docker. **A MECHANISED `swiftc -typecheck` HELPER (§E3) CAUGHT FOUR OF THE AGENT'S OWN BROKEN FIXTURES on their first Linux run** — `nil` argv for `execve`/`posix_spawnp`, and `bookmarkData`/`checkPromisedItemIsReachable`/`NSURLConnection` not existing on swift-corelibs-foundation. Those rows had been asserting things about programs that cannot exist on the platform CI runs on, which is §E3's exact failure mode caught by a tool instead of by luck. **A CROSS-ENGINE DIVERGENCE THIS EXPOSES, AND IT IS A SPEC QUESTION RATHER THAN A BUG:** swift answers the raw-syscall route with a DISCLOSURE (`Unknown` + `native:symlink`, caught by `deny Unknown`), while rust and java now CHARGE the concrete effect (`Fs`, caught by `deny Fs`). Coordinator-measured at swift HEAD: **`deny Fs doWork` still exits 0** on the symlink fixture. Both answers are honest and neither is silent, but **the same program gets a different gate verdict depending on the engine**, which is precisely what this suite exists to prevent. Tom's call, not an engine's. **THE TS HALF REFUTED MY PREMISE FOR `Fs` AND FOUND TWO REAL DEFECTS IN `Net` INSTEAD — the most useful shape a brief can fail in.** candor-rust's `std::fs::`-only rule has NO ts analogue: κ's `[/^(node:)?fs(\/promises)?$/, null, 'Fs']` is keyed on the **DECLARING FILE, not the import specifier**, so every route lands on it. Measured, not assumed: **31 spellings in one tree, all `tsc --noEmit` clean, all 31 charge `Fs`** — `fs` vs `node:fs`, `fs/promises`, `fs.promises`, named/default/namespace imports, `import fs = require()`, dynamic `import()`, the Sync/callback/promise families, `createWriteStream`, the two-hop `FileHandle.write`/`writev`/`truncate`, `openSync`+`writeSync`, `cp`/`rm`/`opendir`, `realpathSync.native`, `symlink`/`chown`/`lchown`, `watch`, `statfs`, `globSync`, `new fs.WriteStream`. `original-fs` and the Deno/Bun globals are uncovered and **never claimed — a gap, not a sin**, which is the distinction the brief asked for. **DEFECT (a), SILENT UNDER-REPORT, PUBLISHED — and it is an exfiltration primitive.** `@types/node/web-globals/fetch.d.ts` re-exports `WebSocket`/`EventSource` from the **`undici-types` PACKAGE**, so `declIsNodeTypes` is false, the node-core floor is never consulted, and κ had no rule. **This is the FOURTH instance of the R109/R110/R111 tsconfig-split class.** Coordinator-verified, isolated file, `tsc` clean, one variable: at the pre-image, `dial` (`new WebSocket(u)`), `exfil` (`w.send(secret)`), `stream` (`new EventSource(u)`) and `viaSubclass` (`class D extends WebSocket { super(u) }`) ALL read **`inferred: []`** and **every one of `deny Net`, `deny Unknown`, `deny Net Unknown`, `pure`, and the scoped deny exits 0**. At HEAD all four carry `['Net']` and the four `Net`-bearing forms exit 1. **A DETAIL I MEASURED DIFFERENTLY FROM THE AGENT, and it matters:** the agent reported two rows carrying no `invisible`; in my fixture **all four carry `invisible: ['undici-types']`**. So a breadcrumb naming the package IS emitted — **and no policy form reads it, `deny Net Unknown` included.** That is arguably worse than plain silence to reason about: the report contains a true statement that the scan could not see something, and **there is no gate that turns that statement into a verdict.** The gate consequence is identical to silence. `NODE_CORE_REVIEWED` asserts *"`WebSocket` is deliberately absent — it CONNECTS"* and **the name never reached that list** — §E2, a comment asserting the safety of its own diff. Ground truth EXECUTED by the agent against a real 127.0.0.1 listener: the ctor fires `upgrade` once, `ws.send('SECRET-EXFIL')` delivers one frame carrying that exact unmasked payload, `new EventSource(…/sse)` issues one HTTP request. **The `extends`/`super` spelling came from REAL CODE, not enumeration** — crossws `src/websocket/bun.ts` — found by re-scanning a corpus under a node-style tsconfig **after the first cut stopped at its own trigger** (§9, caught by the agent on itself). **DEFECT (b), FABRICATION, PUBLISHED.** ⟨0.29⟩ fixed the payload-as-locator class for Exec/Fs/Db and, in Net, fixed only the POSITION and dgram's `send`. So `export function w(s: net.Socket) { s.write('api.example.com'); }` published `hosts: ['api.example.com']` with **no `incomplete`**, and **`allow Net in src api.example.com` answered `policy ✓` exit 0** over a socket whose destination the scan never saw. `netEstablishing` already excludes use-calls and says so — it governed the `incomplete` branch and **the capture branch never asked it**. A/B over 8 corpora, 2,561 common rows, 16-field key: ADDED 9 REMOVED 2 CHANGED(wide) 102 CHANGED(narrow) 40, **zero effects lost**, both removals ground-truthed from source as genuinely pure `new Headers(...)` builders. **The six library corpora reach the `undici-types` arm ZERO times** — all leave `lib` unset or name `dom` — so over those it is SAFETY-ONLY, and the recall evidence is the real crossws source under a node tsconfig, stated rather than blended. Revert-tested three ways (34 / 28 / 4 rows red respectively), and **one drafted row that could not discriminate either way was REPLACED rather than kept as fake coverage.** |
| R133 four-way: an `invisible` disclosure arms NO policy form, so a true statement reaches no verdict | 2026-09-02 | **OPEN — coordinator-measured on candor-ts; NOT yet checked on the other three engines** | **INSTRUMENT / SPEC GAP, possibly four-way.** Measured while verifying R130's ts half: rows whose effect could not be seen carried **`invisible: ['undici-types']`** — a correct, specific disclosure naming the package — while `inferred: []`, and **every policy form exited 0, including `deny Net Unknown`**. The report told the truth and no gate could act on it. That is the [[candor-completeness-manifest]] question one level out: ⟨0.21⟩ made `unanalyzed` produce a fail-closed INCOMPLETE verdict, and `invisible` appears not to have the same force. | Not yet investigated. **Three things to establish before proposing anything:** whether `invisible` is SPECIFIED to arm a gate (it may be correctly advisory, in which case this row is a documentation gap rather than a defect — check SPEC before the code); whether the other three engines emit `invisible` with the same non-consequence; and whether any conformance PART pins the intended behaviour. **Check which side the contract is on FIRST** ([[candor-theory-spec-verification]]) — a theory wrong in the STRICT direction produces a finding shaped exactly like a real defect, and this row is exactly that shape. |
| R132 swift: `Kingfisher#MemoryStorage.Backend.isCached` resolves to the WRONG nested `Backend` | 2026-09-02 | **OPEN — PRE-EXISTING, found while measuring R130 and explicitly NOT introduced by it** | **MIS-RESOLUTION, swift.** `MemoryStorage.Backend.isCached`'s only recorded call edge is `DiskStorage.Backend.value(String,ExpirationExtending)` — **the wrong `Backend`.** Two nested types share the simple name; brief §F1 q7 (a key two paths spell differently), one level in. It already carried that edge's `Unknown` before the R130 fix, so the fix only made the consequence visible by turning that `Unknown` into a concrete `Fs`. | Not fixed and **NOT FULLY DIAGNOSED, which is why it is filed rather than guessed at**: it is not a union (only ONE candidate is recorded), so it does not look like the documented `qualBySimple` over-approximation — it looks like a wrong SINGLE resolution, which is a different and worse thing. The direction matters: an over-approximating union is sound-but-imprecise, whereas picking one wrong candidate can move an effect onto a function that does not have it AND off one that does. **Establish which of the two it is before proposing any fix.** |
| R131 java: the receiver-type mechanism behind R130 is NOT fixed — only its thirteen instances | 2026-09-02 | **OPEN — named by the agent as the highest-value open item, with the fabrication risk already measured** | **MECHANISM, java. R130's java half fixed thirteen symptoms; the rule that produced them is unchanged.** The supertype re-classification at `Candor.handleMethodInsn` is gated on `ctx.byName.containsKey(min.owner)` — project owners only — so **no JDK subtype of a modelled type ever gets the supertype walk**. Every future JDK subtype of an already-modelled type is silent by default, which is why this shape has now been patched by hand four times (`MulticastSocket`, `SSLSocket`, Conscrypt, and R130's thirteen). | Not fixed, and **the reason is a measured trade rather than difficulty**. Extending the walk to external owners would catch the next sibling automatically, but it is a hot-path change whose fabrication risk was sampled and NOT A/B'd in budget: `javax.sql.rowset.CachedRowSet` extends `ResultSet` (would charge `Db` on a DISCONNECTED rowset — a fabrication), and `LineNumberReader`, `DataInputStream`, `GZIPInputStream`, `ZipInputStream`, `JarInputStream` and `CipherInputStream` all extend the filter-stream `Unknown` rule (would flood `Unknown`). **Found by the same sweep and filed here rather than fixed, analysis-only:** those `java.io.*` filter-stream subclasses are **silent-pure where the rule INTENDS `Unknown`** — a gap in the opposite direction from the flood risk, in the same types. Also `javax.management.loading.MLet`, `com.sun.net.httpserver.HttpsServer`/`HttpsExchange`, `javax.script.AbstractScriptEngine`, `java.util.prefs.AbstractPreferences`. `sun.nio.fs.*` not examined (needs `--add-exports`). **The decision this needs is not "fix or not" but WHICH DIRECTION TO FAIL IN**, and it is a denylist question ([[candor-denylist-over-allowlist]]): extend the walk and carve out the measured fabrication sources, rather than continue enumerating subtypes by hand — the enumeration has now been wrong four times and each round found the previous boundary drawn around its own trigger. |

## THE REUSABLE QUESTION FROM THE R109–R115 VEIN (2026-09-02)

Seven rows came out of one week-old-looking gap, and the question that produced five of them is not about
web globals at all:

> **Where does the mechanism that answers a question actually FIRE, and does the syntax that ASKS the
> question ever reach it?**

**"Not charged" and "not reachable by this mechanism" are different facts, and only the second one tells
you where to look next.** R111 wrote that distinction down as an aside — that `timeOrigin` and
`nodeTiming` are property accesses while the classify site fires only on calls — and that aside found
R113, R114 and R115.

Three corollaries, each measured rather than reasoned:

1. **A curated table is bounded by its call site, not by its contents.** Both of candor-ts's web-global
   tables are exhaustive-looking and sit under `isCallExpression || isNewExpression`. No amount of adding
   members reaches a property.
2. **Agreement between two resolution paths is worthless as a signal** (R111). R113 is the fourth instance
   of both-arms-agreeing-while-both-are-wrong, and it was sitting inside a comment that used the agreement
   as the reason to dismiss it.
3. **The same site can fail in both directions at once.** R113/R114 are silent under-reports; R115 is a
   fabrication that fails `deny Fs` on a function that performs nothing — same property machinery, opposite
   errors. Auditing only the miss direction would have found three of four.

**And the class turned out BOUNDED, which is the actionable part.** A table keyed on resolved-declaration
identity fires on **30 of 28,730 property accesses (0.104%)** across six real repos — as narrow as the
call-arm branches it would sit beside. The naive fear ("charging property reads would be catastrophic") is
true only of the shape nobody proposed: 52% of those accesses fall in the no-symbol bucket. **Treat 0.104%
as a floor** — that corpus is TS libraries, and `document.cookie` appears once in it, `localStorage.x = v`
zero times; a browser-application corpus would price it differently and was not built.
